9. **Real decoder — `plugins/camera/gstreamer/frame_capture_gstreamer.py` and `app/perception/frame_capture_factory.py`.** Use GStreamer appsink, the decoded-frame output, with `sync=false` so the receiver clock does not delay delivery; keep source sequence, PTS and per-open identity. Pin `rtspsrc` `buffer-mode=none` (RTP, the media packet clock, only), `ntp-sync=false` and `rfc7273-sync=false`; record these and the fixed jitter-buffer latency. Verify sender intervals survive server/decoder; no receipt retimestamping. Set `detector.camera_index` explicitly to RTSP in a HITL encoded profile; use the real detector/tracker. Test `test_encoded_capture.py` for interval preservation, duplicates and disconnect. Done: 10 minutes, 0 fallbacks/duplicate LOS updates; disconnect/reset aborts and needs new run admission.
   *Built 2026-11-02, live 600 s and disconnect checks passed under other chats' load, with limits below* (`plugins/camera/gstreamer/frame_capture_gstreamer.py`, `encoded_source.py`, `encoded_frame_gate.py`; `FrameSourceIdentity` on `CaptureStamp.source`; profile `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded`; live check `tools/hitl/encoded_capture_check.py`). Source `gstreamer+rtsp://HOST:PORT/PATH?decoder=NAME&latency_ms=N` must state decoder (`nvh265dec` or `avdec_h265`) and latency; the factory opens it only through GStreamer, with no fallback. Pipeline: `rtspsrc protocols=udp buffer-mode=none ntp-sync=false rfc7273-sync=false udp-reconnect=false latency=N ! rtph265depay ! h265parse ! decoder ! videoconvert ! appsink sync=false max-buffers=4 drop=false`. The pins are read back from the elements, logged once per open, and a mismatch refuses the run; `udp-reconnect=false` (Codex) stops rtspsrc from reconnecting by itself. Each published frame carries its PTS, its sequence among this open's accepted frames, and the open's UUID; the stamp stays READ (pull time), with no retiming. The publisher's frame numbers do not cross the stream, so PTS joins to its records. A repeated PTS or a concealed (corrupted) picture is dropped and counted; a repeat does not count as a live stream. A backward PTS, a frame without PTS or a size change is a reset. Before the first frame a refused connection is retried every 1 s for up to 300 s (MediaMTX refuses readers until the publisher is up); after it, end of stream, an error or 5 s without a new PTS ends the open, the capture worker records the failure, and nothing reopens it. These three times are my values: 5 s is rtspsrc's own UDP timeout, and step 8 saw single gaps up to 2.6 s. `DetectionLoop` now skips a frame sequence it already ran, for every real capture: at 30 Hz detection on a 30 fps stream it could otherwise run one picture twice. `test_encoded_capture.py` (30 tests, 2 with real GStreamer in WSL) covers uneven intervals and gaps, identity, repeats, concealed pictures, resets, end/error/silence, no reopen, retries and deadline, pins and their readback, close, and one measurement per picture. Live 2026-11-02 (Unreal, step 8's publisher at 1920 x 1080, ZR10 emulator, the real ChArUco detector and the custom tracker in a WSL venv, aircraft and gimbal state stubbed, CPU share held, other chats' work at 97% CPU): a 600.3 s run passed with 9828 decoded frames, every PTS interval equal to the sender's within one RTP tick per side (worst 10.8 us), 0 sender frames missing between first and last, 0 repeats, 0 fallbacks, 7317 LOS updates with 0 repeated pictures or times; Unreal sent only 16.4 fps under that load (its missed slots, not decoder loss). Disconnect: stopping the publisher aborted the capture after 1.1 s (D1) and, with the final code, 0.59 s on end of stream (D2); a second publisher was live for the last 9 s of 60 s watched, and the capture neither reopened nor took a frame. Four other 600 s attempts ended early and are not explained by the passing run: Unreal's window closed by an unknown hand at 365 s; the publisher's encoder limit at 105 s (todo 185); and twice no new PTS for 5 s while the sender kept sending (300 s with 97 repeated PTS; 128 s with the encoder limit lifted, 94 repeats), cause unproven (todo 227). Limits: the aircraft is a stub and no SITL ran (runner, step 13); the tracker is `custom` because boxmot is not in the WSL venv; system cv2 4.6 lacks `CharucoDetector`, so the decoder side needs a venv (todo 228); the 600 s run predates `udp-reconnect=false`, which changes only a closed control connection; no new run was admitted after a disconnect, since run admission is a later step. Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-02.
10. **Capture clock — `core/kernel/runtime_time.py` and camera/telemetry receipt adapters.** Use runtime-bound `rt.perf_counter`, a high-resolution clock that never steps backward, for local capture, attitude/gimbal receipts, histories and deadlines. Add a separate capture receipt stamp; keep wall-clock fields used by unrelated telemetry. Test `test_capture_clock.py` with wall-clock jumps and a rebound test runtime. Done: 0 mixed-clock subtractions; reported and observed tick spacing ≤0.1 ms under load. Raw PC/Orin epochs are never assumed equal.
   *Built 2026-11-02, unit level only; merged 2026-11-02* The capture clock is `rt.perf_counter`. The MAVLink router stamps each message twice at receipt: the wall `receipt_time_s`, kept for telemetry, and `capture_receipt_s`; ATTITUDE samples carry both. On the capture clock: camera READ stamps (ffmpeg, OpenCV, GStreamer, NPYC) and the GStreamer first-frame and silence deadlines; `AttitudeBoardClock` (renamed `capture_time_s`) and so NPYC EXPOSURE stamps; frame publication and its freshness; attitude interpolation (a sample without a capture receipt is refused, `real_frame_pose_capture_receipt_unavailable`) and association; the real detection's measurement and receipt "now" providers; detection/track freshness; the Kalman and boxmot trackers; SIYI attitude and zoom receipts (vendored `siyi_sdk.py` now reads `rt.perf_counter`, its SOLID hash pin renewed); gimbal loss recovery, whose fallback time for a lost real detection was wall time and the mount state's zoom age. Renamed to match: `attitude_lookup_s`, `CaptureTiming.lookup_s`, `FramePublicationStore(clock=)`. The step 8/9 check tools (`encoded_capture_check.py`, `npyc_live_check.py`) moved to `perf_counter` with the frames. `test_capture_clock.py` rebinds the runtime to a clock whose wall time jumps by hours and whose monotonic ticks at 15.6 ms on another epoch: ATTITUDE receipt, board-time mapping, EXPOSURE publication, association, READ stamps, track freshness, SIYI receipts and gimbal loss timing stay exact, and a scan finds no `time`/`monotonic` read in the 22 capture-path modules (it reads text only; consumers were traced by hand); putting the wall clock back in association, the board clock or freshness fails it. Tick spacing at 100% CPU with four busy threads: reported 0.1 us, observed median 0.1 us, p99 0.2 us (the test bounds p99), max 12 us (thread preemption, not clock resolution); `monotonic` ticks at 15.6 ms. Left on their own clocks, consistent within themselves: the inference loop cadence (`monotonic`), the VISCA zoom timeout (wall), the simulated gimbal's zoom age (`monotonic`), and the sim pose pairing (wall receipts of sim truth).
11. **Explicit integration timing — `core/perception/pipeline/real_frame_association.py` and `camera/integration_timing.py`.** Build a HITL-only estimate, never a fabricated `CaptureLookupCalibration`. Reuse `board_clock_map.py`'s lower envelope (the smallest recent read-minus-PTS offset): map PTS to receiver read time from the latest 2 seconds, refreshing at most once per unique frame; freeze window/update/slip limits in the run record. For each source, add its separately fitted receiver-local correction C = Ds − Dc (source-link minus video delay); do not claim separate absolute delays. Keep PTS intervals for visual rates; mapping updates never become frame intervals. Log the actual associated attitude `time_boot` and gimbal sample identities/weights. Refuse unbracketed (no sample on each side), stale or unsettled state; a reset or offset jump >1 ms aborts. Test `test_integration_timing.py` with jitter, ±50/±300 ppm (clock-rate error), 10-minute traces and a slip. Done: fixture mapping error ≤1 ms, every slip refused, 0 read-difference law intervals or flight/Qualification admissions; all uncertified, no Unreal/scorer metadata in navpy.
   *Built 2026-11-02, unit level only; merged 2026-11-03* (`core/perception/camera/integration_timing.py`, `pts_read_map.py` and `integration_slip.py`, `core/perception/pipeline/integration_association.py`, `core/ports/gimbal_capture_history.py`; run record schema 3). The map follows `board_clock_map.py`: R is the smallest read-minus-PTS of the newest 2 s of unique frames; the published offset O moves toward R by at most 0.1 ms per unique frame (a repeated PTS changes nothing), and a frame maps to `pts + O` with the O of its own publication. Every publication feeds the map once (the frame store's observer). Lookups add each source's correction C = Ds − Dc: attitude at `mapped + C_a`, gimbal at `mapped + C_g`, both strictly bracketed by adjacent samples; no absolute delay is claimed, and ages taken from `mapped` are short by the smallest video delay. Slip checks use a drift-corrected floor: its rate is the lower-hull line fit (the linear-programming clock-skew estimate of Moon, Skelly and Towsley) over the 10 s before the two newest 1 s slip windows, its level the rate-corrected minimum of that same window (the hull line itself; the minimum of one 1 s window rode up to 0.1 ms on jitter and let 17 of 864 slips of 1.1 ms hidden in a gap through, when gaps still resettled). A frame more than 1 ms below the floor, a newest slip window more than 1 ms above it, R more than 1 ms from O, another open, a backward PTS or a PTS gap over 0.5 s aborts the open for good. A first try fitted the rate over 2 s and false-aborted one 10-minute trace at +300 ppm with 2 ms jitter: 2 s of jittered minima cannot tell 0.3 ms/s from noise. Nothing maps until 12 s of frames (rate window plus two slip windows, at least 30 frames) back the checks; the slip check also runs on the frame that first settles the map. A gap ends the open because the fitted rate is off by up to 35 ppm under 2 ms jitter (p99.9 0.94 to 35 ppm over 60 two-minute traces), so a floor carried across a 30 s gap cannot tell a 1.1 ms slip from rate error (Codex round 3). Two earlier tries resettled after a gap and each let a slip through. Fail-closed: a stalled stream ends integration timing for that open. Visual rates use the PTS axis: `interval_timestamp_s` goes from `CaptureTiming.interval_s` through `PixelObservation` and `SourceTiming` to `FinalApproachVisionFrame.rate_time_s`, which every `vision_pn` difference and the gimbal rate tracker now read; refused frames carry their PTS too, so a tracker never mixes the two axes; measurement age and ordering stay on mapped time. Admission: `IntegrationTimingConfig` refuses any mode but `pc-sitl` (so flight and Qualification), empty ids, non-finite corrections and inconsistent limits; the builder refuses a capture calibration beside it; the association refuses a source or live link other than the config's (`real_frame_integration_context_mismatch`); a frame whose association ends after the capture thread aborted the map is refused like every later one; everything it admits carries `integration_uncertified`, which `is_certified` never accepts. Refusals, each named: unmapped, unsettled or aborted frame, frame older than 0.5 s, newest attitude or gimbal receipt older than 0.2 s, attitude or gimbal unbracketed or a gap (gimbal readbacks more than 0.1 s apart), gimbal history unavailable, and any zoom-capable mount (`real_frame_zoom_unsettled`: no settled-zoom evidence before step 18). A fixed-lens gimbal is admitted with its angles interpolated at the capture instant (`gimbal_capture_relative`). The SIYI store keeps a 64-sample history per connection session, a sample kept only when its receipt is later and its 16-bit sequence newer (wrap-aware); the snapshot's own rule is unchanged, since the real ZR10's sequence behaviour is unmeasured. Each association attempt writes one record (run, open, sequence, PTS, read, mapped, O, R, rate, attitude lookup, bracketing `time_boot`s, receipts and weight, interpolated `time_boot`, gimbal lookup, session, sequences, receipts and weight, zoom sample, status). The run record freezes the corrections and all limits. `test_integration_timing.py` (60 tests): 10-minute traces at ±50 and ±300 ppm with 2 ms mean jitter map within 1 ms with 0 aborts; slips of ±1.2 ms (no drift or jitter), ±1.5 ms (±300 ppm, 1 ms jitter) and ±5 ms (±50 ppm, 2 ms jitter) all abort, with nothing admitted after the abort; reset, repeat, settling and gap, a gap over 0.5 s aborts as a reset (0.6 and 30 s, with and without a ±1.05 ms slip, also right after the first admitted frame); a 0.4 s gap keeps mapping at ±300 ppm, and a ±1.1 ms slip inside it aborts under 2 ms jitter; PTS intervals exact while O moves; admission refusals; association bracket identities, weights and log records; each refusal named and logged; refused frames keep the PTS axis; an abort during association refuses the frame; aircraft yaw does not change the result; gimbal history reorder, duplicate, wrap and reconnect; law rate states on PTS. A wider synthetic sweep (not in the suite): 45 ten-minute traces at ±300, ±50 and 0 ppm with 0.2-2 ms mean jitter, 0 aborts, worst mapping error 0.60 ms (R trails a +300 ppm floor by up to 2 s × 0.3 ms/s); 360 slip traces of ±1.1, ±1.2, ±1.5 and ±5 ms at ±300 and 0 ppm with 0-2 ms jitter, all aborted, none early, downward within 0.77 s, upward within 1.00 s; 180 traces with a gap under the limit (0.1-0.45 s after 12.1 or 20 s, ±300, ±50 and 0 ppm, 0-2 ms jitter), 0 aborts, worst mapping error 0.64 ms, and 648 traces with a ±1.1, ±1.5 or ±5 ms slip inside such a gap, all aborted. Limits: frames of an upward slip published before its abort (up to 1 s, about 30 frames) were admitted with up to the jump as error (owner ruling 2026-11-02); the 1 s slip window replaces the owner's "about 0.25 s", which false-aborted under ms jitter; a PTS gap over 0.5 s ends integration timing for that open (fail-closed); all limit values (0.1 ms update, 1 s slip window, 10 s rate window, 30 frames, 0.5 s gap and frame age, 0.2 s source age, 0.1 s gimbal bracket) are my synthetic choices, not measured; the 2 s envelope keeps up to 0.6 ms of mapping error at 300 ppm; no live run (the runner is step 13, the fits step 12); with the ZR10 emulator's zoom, no frame is admitted until step 18. SOLID guard ratchets, one field or method each: `RealFrameAssociation` 18 and `FinalApproachVisionFrame` 16 fields (`interval_timestamp_s`), `CameraMount` 45 and `GimbalSiyi` 23 methods (capture history). Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-03.
12. **Offline timing validity — `tools/hitl/check_integration_timing.py`.** Build log joins by stream/session and PTS/sequence, including actual Unreal render stamps and gimbal brackets; finish step 17's producer logging before the held-out check. On ≥30 calibration-only pairs, fit each C directly from receiver-local receipt histories: interpolate ATTITUDE receipts at the represented board time, and gimbal receipts at the rendered source sample identities/weights. Only constants enter navpy; no PC/WSL/Orin epoch subtraction. Test known board-time/sample joins, missing identities, drift and slips. Done: held-out 10-minute real-SITL observation on each receiver, and every approach reports median/p95/max skew: associated attitude `time_boot` minus frame board time, and associated versus rendered gimbal time on its source clock (emulator in stages 1-2, Orin tap from stage 3). Max skew plus join/fit uncertainty ≤8 ms for both; minimum represented interval ≥10× max PTS interval error including uncertainty. Missing/ambiguous/over-budget joins fail. These checks are needed to interpret integration; exposure certificates, absolute delay/host-clock bounds and true pointing stay in Qualification.
   *Built 2026-11-03, unit level only; no live run; merged 2026-11-03* (`tools/hitl/check_integration_timing.py`, `timing_inputs.py`, `timing_joins.py`, `timing_run.py`, `timing_fit.py`, `timing_check.py`, `timing_stats.py`; producer logging in `integration_log.py`, `integration_association.py` and `zr10_emulator.py`, step 17's part). `fit` and `check` take one receiver, one stream and one run each: navpy's integration log, the publisher's `frames-NNN.csv`, Unreal's `NavPyCaptureSlot` lines, the emulator's JSONL and the run record. Joins, all by identity: navpy frame to publisher frame by PTS delta from one anchor within one RTP tick per side (navpy's PTS has its own zero); exactly one anchor may fit every frame, since a sender frame lost on the way leaves no trace in navpy's sequence; publisher frame to Unreal sent slot by frame index and board time, one camera run, generation and system, with a valid pose, clock and gimbal bracket; slot to emulator steps by gimbal session and bracket sequences and weight (rendered plant time); navpy readback to emulator 0x0D frame by SIYI wire sequence, both unwrapped, one unique offset, one destination, with the whole emulator stream numbered one by one. navpy now logs each ATTITUDE and gimbal receipt it sees once, before the first association record that could see it, and each record names its now, its history snapshot bounds and the attitude bracket and seam limits it ran with; NaN and Infinity, or a malformed association field, are input errors; the emulator logs every SIYI frame it sends (`siyi_out`: sequence, command, destination, device time, and for 0x0D the plant time it reports, report lag applied). Fit: attitude pairs = receipts interpolated at the rendered board time minus mapped; gimbal pairs = readback receipts interpolated at the rendered plant time over the reported plant times minus mapped; an attitude pair counts only where navpy's own `interpolate_attitude_at`, with the run's logged bracket, seam and quarantine limits, accepts the same bracket (a sample without board time stays a seam); a gimbal bracket is two adjacent readbacks with rising plant time inside the run's 0.1 s. C is the median of at least 30 pairs in at least 3 occupied 10 s bins; its uncertainty is the larger distance from the median to a 95% block bootstrap interval that redraws whole occupied 10 s bins, each with the same chance whatever its pair count (2000 resamples). Check: every mapped frame must have both results; attitude skew = associated `time_boot` minus board time; gimbal skew = associated readback plant time minus rendered plant time. Until step 18 every zoom-capable mount is refused before the gimbal lookup, so the check replays navpy's `interpolate_gimbal_at` on the snapshot that attempt logged; a live association must equal its replay. Pass: max |skew| + fit uncertainty + join resolution (1 ms for ATTITUDE, whose `time_boot` is whole ms; 0 for gimbal) ≤ 8 ms per source; smallest represented interval ≥ 10 × (largest PTS interval error + one RTP tick); the frozen corrections equal the constants, the fitted uncertainty is finite and not negative, and every record's lookup minus mapped equals the corrections; the checked run is not a fit run; one open; no map interruption after the first mapped frame. `--observation` adds ≥ 600 s scored per source and no gap between scored frames over 1.0 s. Missing, ambiguous or invalid joins, unreadable records and over-budget skews fail. `test_check_integration_timing.py` (42 tests) on a synthetic world with known epochs, render lag, 30 fps, missed slots, a 16-bit wrap and report lag: the fit recovers each C; known board-time and sample joins; held-out pass; live versus replay; ±300 ppm drift; a 2 ms slip passes and ±12 ms fail; heavy jitter fails; an abort after a good prefix fails; frames without results after a good prefix fail; NaN fields and a non-finite or negative uncertainty fail; a sample without board time and a 30 ms seam drop pairs as live; changing limits are invalid; blocks count only occupied bins, every bin is drawn alike and the interval reflects correlation within blocks; an anchor implying fewer lost frames is still ambiguous; the 600 s rule and a 10-minute pass; held-out and frozen-constant rules; too few pairs; and each missing, ambiguous or invalid join. Limits: no held-out real-SITL observation has run: no runner wires `IntegrationTimingConfig` and the log into navpy (step 13), and a wall run needs SITL, bridge, Unreal, publisher, MediaMTX and emulator under the CPU-share rule; gimbal skew is a replay of navpy's own function until step 18 admits zoom; a receiver window that crosses no sender gap is ambiguous and fails, since every navpy gap may be a frame lost on the way (Unreal's uneven output gives gaps in real runs) (a transport identity such as the RTP timestamp base logged on both sides would remove this; not built); one gimbal session and one open per check (a reconnect needs a new check); the binding of the files to one run is by run id and session only: a file set that is consistently from another run, Unreal system or emulator destination is not caught, and a log cut after whole lines is not detected; both need step 13's manifest (Codex round 1, left open for the owner). My values, not the plan's: the median fit, block bootstrap over 10 s bins (the slip rate window), 2000 resamples and seed, 3 occupied bins minimum, 1 ms ATTITUDE resolution, 1.0 s coverage gap, the 64-frame anchor probe, and refusing any mapped frame without a result. Owner accepted the merge with these values and limits on 2026-11-03; the held-out 10-minute real-SITL observation moves to step 13.
13. **Approach runner — `tools/tuning/approach.py` and `sitl_driver.py`.** Build the new `approach` subcommand, initially `pc-sitl`. Reuse `hitl.py` `sitl-backing`, `hitl_procs` ownership, dock-pass takeoff setup, `NavpyHandoff`/`HeightFloor` and the `aas-hitl` publish CLI. Start backing → bridge → router → emulator → Unreal → publisher → navpy with readiness checks; fly only the dock-independent search lane, enable acquisition, and recover/RTL (return to launch) only after quiesce. Stop owned processes on every exit. Test `test_approach_runner.py` with fake processes, duplicate owners and startup failures. Done: 1 end-to-end observation, 1 complete manifest, 0 orphaned processes. Later stages add their named mode.
   *Built 2026-11-03, pc-sitl observation; merged 2026-11-03* (`tools/tuning/approach.py`, `approach_chain.py`, `approach_procs.py`, `approach_handles.py`, `approach_lane.py`; `sitl_driver approach`; navpy `--siyi-ip`, `app/perception/siyi_address_override.py`). Chain and ports: `sitl-backing` instance 240 with the sim-hil binary (the default flight-tune binary is HIL contract v1, a follow-up) → bridge (pose to Unreal, vehicle-state copy to the Windows emulator) → `mav_router` → the ZR10 emulator on Windows (Unreal joins gimbal samples on QPC, so it must stamp on Windows; it stops on end of input, since SIGINT through WSL interop reaches only the proxy, and its Windows Python is watched and killed only as the pid its ready line names with the start time recorded then, checked and killed in one call, never by port) → Unreal → the `aas-hitl` publisher (stopped by `drain` then `stop` on its control socket; SIGINT exits 1) → navpy. Each role has a readiness check and timeout (backing 120 s, router 30, emulator 30, publisher 300, Unreal 240, navpy 180 s, my values), refuses to start on a port another owner holds, and is stopped on every exit: its own stop, then terminate and kill until its whole process group is gone (a step that fails is recorded and the next runs; a failed alive check counts as alive; members left after a handoff stop are ended too); a role the handoff stopped is recorded stopped. A board stop that fails is recorded and the processes still stop. navpy and the publisher start at nice 10 (my value): in fit 8 navpy's decoding starved the bridge (turnaround p99 232 ms, about 90 board state losses, a crash after RTL); WSL lets a user only lower a priority, so section 8's HIGH bridge and router are met by lowering the consumers. Lane (my values): bearing 0°, from 400 m, 2000 m long, 80 m above home, 30 out-and-back repeats; acquisition is enabled on the lane; `NavpyHandoff` and the router barrier quiesce navpy before RTL; `HeightFloor` 30 m. The link-health gate treats a log that begins with a disarm as begun armed (the SITL log starts at the arm and loses its ARM record). Evidence: fit 9 `approach-20261103T163502Z` and fit 10 `approach-20261103T164326Z` (evidence in `.logs/hitl-step13`): error none, 0 orphans, every role ended after its own stop, manifest complete, navpy received frames on the lane, quiesced before RTL; in fit 9 the lowest height was 40.6 m and the worst state age 94 ms. Limits: the link gate fails on stale frames (19.9%; 14.3% is the known SITL pacing loss of 1 loop in 7, `flight_tuning.md:840`), so the runner exits 1; the emulator has no CPU affinity. **Step 12's held-out timing check is deferred (owner, 2026-11-03):** navpy's PTS-to-read map aborts about 12 s in on pc-sitl (fit 10: 3909 aborted, 252 unsettled association records), because the SITL board clock loses real time unevenly under WSL load (publisher receive-minus-PTS grows 93 ms over 200 s, local slopes up to ±3000 ppm) against the map's 1 ms slip limit. The check runs on `pixhawk-hil` or a steady SITL clock; navpy does not log which limit aborted the map (a follow-up). Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-03: the held-out timing check is deferred, and the runner still exits 1 on pc-sitl because the link gate fails on the known SITL stale-frame rate.

A series file in the private folder, written once, freezes 10 secret seeds, the dock region, the consumers, the lane and the limits; the runner takes `--scenario-series` and `--seed-index`. Per seed the service draws the dock's offset (uniform on a disc of 30 m about the level's dock, my value; a region above 200 m is refused) and its yaw (0-360 deg), in whole mm and mdeg, and hashes the scene, seed included, so the public hash cannot be searched back to a pose. The service runs in its own process and writes the run's scenario folder: `scenario.json` (identity, lane, limits; no pose, no seed), one private file per consumer (only Unreal, contact and scoring are allowed; Unreal and scoring are required; contact joins in step 26) and a ledger line per delivery or refusal. The runner reads only `scenario.json`, puts it in the run record (navpy receives identity, lane and limits only through the record), passes Unreal its file's path unread, and leaves the private files out of the manifest. With `-NavPyScenario=` Unreal moves the dock, snaps it, places the board, writes its readback, the snap and the board pose to `private/unreal_applied.json`, and logs only the scene hash and relative terrain heights (the runner reads `unreal.log`); a file it cannot apply, a board that is turned off or not placed, and a failed terrain sample are refused, with no applied file. Scoring rebuilds the scene from Unreal's readback and requires the delivered hash, the scene's level as Unreal loaded it, a complete board readback, the dock within 0.05 m of the terrain (my value), no pose line in Unreal's log, and a ledger where every declared consumer got exactly one delivery with its file and nothing else, all for the scene `scenario.json` names; the runner starts navpy only once Unreal is ready, which with a scenario means its camera is mounted and it logged the run's scene hash as applied (a refusal ends the wait), and accepts a verdict only on its own scene hash; a run exits 1 otherwise. The runner's `--commission-fault` (`none`, `delay:N[@K]`, `loss[@K]`; default `none`) writes the fault into the run record's `commissioning` section; only mode `pc-sitl` may carry one (`COMMISSIONING_MODES`), and the record reader refuses a malformed fault or one in another mode. With a fault, the runner starts navpy through `tools.hitl.media_fault_injector`, which refuses unless the real detector runs with `--integration-timing` and `--integration-log` and the record names an active fault in a commissioning mode, then hands navpy a wrapper object (no CLI option: navpy's `VisionArgs.capture_wrapper` is set only by that launcher, and `src` imports no `tools`). navpy wraps only the decoded encoded stream (`gstreamer+rtsp://`; other sources and push mode are refused), closes the backend if wrapping fails, and logs the wrap. The wrapper passes the first K decoded frames, then under loss drops every frame, and under delay holds frames in a deque of N+1 and publishes the oldest when more than N are held, as the same frame and `CaptureStamp` objects (PTS, open id and sequence kept, read time unchanged), right after its age check; a frame older than `max_hold_s` on its own stamp is discarded, never published late. One open per run; a JSONL fault log (`media_fault.jsonl`, one line per frame, a close summary) is in the run's manifest, ended by its close line; it is optional, so ordinary runs are unchanged. 16. **Observation and shutdown — `tools/tuning/approach.py` and `app/terminal_handoff.py`.** Reuse the router, `NavpyHandoff` and recovery floor; add idempotent (safe to repeat) quiesce for terminal, deadline, crash and abort. Observation blocks all navpy aircraft writes while the harness flies only the fixed search lane. Test `test_approach_observation.py` and `test_approach_quiesce.py`, including late queues/reconnect. Done: 0 forbidden observation writes and 0 writes after drain acknowledgment in every fault case; deadlines never manufacture DOCKED.
   *Built 2026-11-04, live pc-sitl runs and unit level; merged 2026-11-04* (`tools/tuning/approach_quiesce.py`; `approach.py`; router `mav_router*.py` reader client and write log; navpy `app/terminal_handoff.py`, `app/runtime_composition.py`). Observation: navpy joins the router as a reader (`--reader navpy=PORT`); only `observation_allowed` messages reach the board (gimbal/SIYI commands, stream claims and REQUEST_MESSAGE stay allowed), and the router logs every client write it forwards (stamp, source, message id, command) to `router_writes.csv`. pc-sitl is always in observation; command mode comes in step 27. Quiesce: one `Quiesce` per run wraps `NavpyHandoff.before_rtl`; the first call stops navpy and closes its router endpoint (barrier on the control port), later calls only add their reason. Every end runs it before the board's stop: navpy's terminal record, the deadline, the floor, a crash and an abort (Ctrl+C, SIGTERM, SIGHUP); crash and abort send no RTL. Signals: SIGTERM and SIGHUP raise `Aborted` during a run; the cleanup ignores SIGINT, SIGTERM and SIGHUP (blocked while the handlers change, retried past one on its way) and restores them after; a second signal in the same microseconds as the first, before the block, stays a limit. navpy owns its quiesce from the handoff's creation (a failed vehicle build, whose own rollback runs first, still quiesces), and `TerminalHandoff.join()` is true only once its record is published. `observe()` ends on navpy's record only when a complete line is read before the deadline; `outcome()` counts it only then and only with trigger `terminal`, so a deadline, stop, floor or crash never yields DOCKED. `audit()` reads the write log: navpy's writes outside the allow list, and after the first drain ack (navpy's own or the runner's), must both be 0, and a missing log or ack fails. My values: handoff join 4 s (gimbal 1 s + barrier 2 s + 1 s), terminal poll 1 s; a record read after the deadline counts as the deadline. Tests: `test_approach_quiesce.py` (40; board boundary for terminal, deadline, crash, abort with a late queue, navpy reconnect and a board reboot; signals sent for real and mid-swap) and `test_approach_observation.py` (37). Every run exits 1 on the board link-health gate (accepted, predates this step). Limits: a navpy that crashes sends no gimbal stop; in both hangup runs navpy exited 1 after its record, drain and gimbal stop, because the SIYI SDK threads did not stop during disconnect (`siyi/hardware/shutdown.py`), a follow-up. Evidence: worktree `.logs/hitl-step16`. Follow-ups are todos 262 and 263. Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-04: crash and abort paths send no RTL, gimbal commands stay allowed in observation, a crashed navpy sends no gimbal stop, and the chosen handoff and poll times stand.
