   *Part built with step 12, 2026-11-03*: the emulator's `siyi_out` records (each sent frame's wire sequence, command, destination and device time; for 0x0D the reported plant time with report lag), tested in `test_zr10_emulator.py`. The ownership checks, `aas-hitl` launcher disabling and `test_approach_owners.py` remain.
   *Built 2026-11-04, live pc-sitl runs and unit level; merged 2026-11-04* (`tools/tuning/approach_owners.py`, `approach_tables.py`, `approach.py`; aas-hitl `aas_hitl/zr10/preflight.py`, `__main__.py`, branch `claude/hitl-step17-emulator-ownership`). Reuse was already in place: the emulator uses navpy's SIYI codec (`siyi_message`) and sim plant (`gimbal_angles`, `gimbal_zoom_plant`) and takes its pose only from the bridge's vehicle_state copy. Owner census: the runner reads WSL's `/proc` (parent, process group, command line; sockets by fd inode) and Windows' `Win32_Process` (with the image name) and `netstat -ano`, and maps each role's footprint to the runner role whose process tree holds it, else foreign: autopilot = TCP listeners 8160 and 8168 and clients of 8160 other than the router; bridge = clients of 8168; emulator = UDP 37260 and 17113 bound on either host; publisher = TCP listeners 18110 and 18554 on either host; and on both hosts the programs `[nice -n N] python|py|pythonw|pyw [options] -m MODULE` for navpy's `tools.tuning.hitl_bridge` and `tools.hitl.zr10_emulator`, and aas-hitl's `aas_hitl.zr10`, `aas_hitl.video.gstreamer_encoder`, `run`, `sitl start|mission`, `bridge`, `zr10 emulate` and `video publish`. wslrelay.exe's Windows mirrors of WSL listeners and a Windows venv launcher (same arguments as its child) are not owners; a socket of an unknown process is foreign; a table that cannot be read fails. Censuses (my choice of times): before any start nothing may hold a role (refused before anything starts); once every role is ready, and again after the quiesce, each role has exactly its own owner, its program at most once per host, and the sockets one process holds together held by one process per host (SITL's 8160 and 8168; the emulator's 37260 and 17113; the publisher's 18110 and 18554 are two processes) (a second copy whose command line Windows hides is caught by its sockets; a failure at ready stops the run; at the end it fails the run and every stop still runs). Log check after the run: Unreal's log has exactly one pose stream generation on 17110 and poses on no other port, exactly one gimbal session, the emulator's, and every sent slot on it; the emulator's summary has 1 bridge session, 0 other systems and 0 board time regressions (its `valid` flag is false in every pc-sitl run, from known vehicle_state gaps, so it is not used). Exit 0 needs all of these. aas-hitl: its ZR10 emulator (`zr10 emulate` and `python -m aas_hitl.zr10`) refuses to start while any other process holds its UDP port on any address, or the table cannot be read; `run`, `sitl start` and `sitl mission` already refuse while navpy's SITL runs, since 8160 is in the demo SITL band (`demo_stopped`; a test pins it); its bridge, Unreal-publishing emulator and publisher already refuse beside navpy's Unreal. Tests: `test_approach_owners.py` (program rule incl. shells that only name a program, `/proc` and netstat and process-list parsing, a running chain with relay mirrors and the venv launcher, 12 conflicting listeners and processes in WSL and on Windows, navpy's and aas-hitl's, a second copy inside a role's own tree, a runner role on another role's port, a missing role, unreadable tables, the three census phases, every log rule), `test_approach_quiesce.py` (a foreign owner before the run starts nothing; one at the end fails the run and every process still stops), `test_integration_timing.py` (a known 40 ms gimbal report lag: the joined gimbal state is the plant's only when C_g holds the lag, 0.16 deg off without it), aas-hitl `tests/zr10/test_siyi_port_guard.py`. Evidence (`.logs/hitl-step17`): all 19 earlier approach runs pass the log check (`historic_sources.json`); live pc-sitl seed 0 `approach-20261104T105955Z`: preflight, ready and end censuses each 1 owner per role and the log check passed, while aas-hitl's emulator was started beside it and refused naming navpy's emulator's pid (`live/`); the two earlier live runs found the wslrelay and shell false owners fixed above. Limits: no live run reached the lane: takeoff failed in the last two runs and in a baseline run of the code before this step (`2f47dfe9`) under the same load (other chats at 96% CPU, 110-203 HIL state losses), so takeoff failure is not from this step, and runs exit 1 on it and on link health; a census sees processes at three instants, so a competitor that starts and exits between them is caught only if it reaches the logs (a second pose sender, gimbal sender or bridge session); a TCP client of remote port 8160 or 8168 on another machine counts as an owner (fails closed); two aas-hitl and navpy emulators starting within the same instant can both bind (no shared lock: aas-hitl stays independent of navpy); a Windows program whose command line hides its module (another launcher or wrapper) is seen only by its ports; a second copy that binds none of the role's ports and runs a hidden command line inside the role's own tree is not seen. Pointing fidelity stays provisional. Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-04.
18. **Fixed zoom policy — `core/perception/zoom/fixed_step_zoom_policy.py` and `command/gimbal/gimbal_zoom_control.py`.** Use calibrated steps, both-axis image-containment margins and hysteresis (separate thresholds to avoid repeated switching); no range-driven zoom. Test `test_approach_zoom.py` for travel, loss of features and unknown readings. Done: 100% of commands select measured steps; 0 unsettled measurements update LOS; an exceeded frozen age/gap limit aborts rather than extending freshness.
   *Built 2026-11-04, unit level only; no live run; merged 2026-11-04* (`core/perception/zoom/fixed_zoom_steps.py`, `zoom_settle_evidence.py`, `fixed_step_zoom_policy.py`, `fixed_step_zoom_drive.py`; `app/perception/fixed_zoom_profile.py`; `command/gimbal/gimbal_zoom_control.py`, `gimbal_navigation_composition.py`; `pipeline/integration_association.py`, `integration_zoom.py`, `integration_log.py`; `command/gimbal/gimbal_detection_lifecycle.py`; `ports/gimbal_capture_history.py`; SIYI `hardware/state.py`, `commands.py`; branch `claude/hitl-step18-fixed-zoom-6262dc`). Steps: the measured `absolute_zoom` rows of the profile's `camera.reading_intrinsics` (one stream mode), widest first; interpolated rows and the wide stop are not steps, two steps may not share a reading, and the profile's `zoom_calibration` must give each step's reading (siyi_zr10: 2.0, 3.0, 4.0, 6.0, 8.0, 9.0, 10.0, reading 1.8 to 10.0). Built only beside integration timing; a mount without a reading table keeps the old refusal (`real_frame_zoom_unsettled`). Settle evidence (one object shared by the association and the drive): the SIYI store now keeps the last 64 zoom readings (session, sequence, `rt.perf_counter` receipt, same newer-only rule as attitude) and the last 64 zoom writes (logged before sending; absolute command in tenths, None for 0x05 manual zoom), both in `GimbalCaptureHistory`. The latest command opens an epoch; arrival is the first reading after it equal to the step's reading; the step is settled once that reading has held for the dwell and the minimum count; a frame at the gimbal lookup t is settled only when t >= arrival + dwell and a held reading arrived at or after t. Statuses refused by name: `real_frame_zoom_travel` (reading not at the step, dwell not met, lookup before the command), `real_frame_zoom_unbracketed`, `real_frame_zoom_unknown` (no command this session, a manual or non-step command, a reading change after settling without a command), `real_frame_zoom_aborted` (reading gap, newest reading too old, or history lost between two snapshots; latched for the gimbal session, a reconnect needs a new command and settle; a snapshot of an older session is refused as `real_frame_zoom_history_unavailable` and cannot reset a newer one), `real_frame_zoom_stream_unmeasured` (frame size not the table's stream). A settled frame takes the step row's K and distortion (read-only), `zoom_command` = the step, `zoom_capture_relative`, so the gate admits it; every association record carries the judgement, and the timing log gains `zoom_reading`, `zoom_command` and one `zoom_config` record. Policy (frame-local box and camera readback only; no range, altitude, ground speed or compass): per axis the gap from the box to the nearer edge as a fraction of the axis; zoom out one step when either gap is below the out margin; zoom in one step when the box predicted at the narrower step (pinhole, each row's fx/fy/cx/cy) keeps both gaps at or above the in margin; between the margins the step holds; the loss count of settled updates without a detection goes to the widest step; unknown commands the widest step; travel, unbracketed and aborted command nothing; a box from another step's frame is not used. The fixed-step drive is the only zoom writer: with fixed zoom no `PoiZoomTracker` is built, which also turns off the range-driven geo acquisition zoom; a failed write is retried before any new decision, but not after a reconnect, and a narrower step not into a freeze, and any explicit widest command voids it; a detection without a usable box makes no containment decision, but an unknown lens still goes widest; tracking starts with fixed zoom alone, without rate tracking; the final-approach freeze commands the widest step and holds it, restoring it only from unknown; after an abort it writes nothing. Tests: `test_approach_zoom.py` (54: steps from synthetic and the shipped ZR10 table, the `zoom_calibration` cross-check; travel at every phase incl. in-bin reading equality before the dwell and a reading leaving before it; unknown commands and readings, off-grid and wrong readings; gap, frozen age and lost history abort and stay aborted; new session; SIYI store and command log; containment out/in/band, a 300-target no-re-entry hysteresis check, loss, status table; end to end at lens speeds inf, 2 and 0.5 per s with 30 ms report lag and 20 ms command latency: every write a step, every admitted frame's true lens still at that step and set by its command, 300+ admitted, under 20 writes in 40 s; frozen drive holds then aborts on a stopped readback; association: settled admitted with the row's K and dist, travel/unknown/aborted/frozen refused and not frame-atomic, stream mismatch, no fixed zoom still refused; Codex round 1: an older-session snapshot cannot clear an abort, a failed write is not retried across a reconnect while frozen, `GimbalNavigation` with fixed zoom and no rate tracker starts tracking and commands the widest step; round 2: a failed zoom-in does not survive a freeze at the widest step), `test_integration_timing.py` unchanged results. Codex review: round 1 found 3 points (P1 session rollback, P2 stale retry, P2 fixed-only tracking never started), all verified and fixed; round 2 confirmed them and found 1 more (P2: the unusable-box path and an already-widest freeze kept a failed zoom-in pending), fixed (an explicit widest command and every update void it); round 3 found nothing open; logs in `.logs/hitl-step18/review`. My values: dwell 0.3 s (proves the lens left its 0.1 bin at any speed above 0.33 per s; the rig measured 1.5 to 4.5), 3 held readings, reading gap 0.5 s and reading age 0.5 s (step 11's frame gap and age), margins out 0.10 and in 0.25, loss 3 settled updates. Limits: no live run (pc-sitl's integration map aborted about 12 s in at step 15, and takeoff failed under load at step 17), so the step 12 gimbal skew check on the live association is still to do; the widest step is 2.0 (the wide stop 1.0 is not a step); no travel timeout: a lens that never reaches its step stays refused (fails closed, no zoom change until loss or unknown); autofocus is not modelled; a lens left at a non-step setting with the same reading as the commanded step, with command latency longer than the dwell, would settle early (in the shipped table only step 8.0's reading 7.7 is shared, with the interpolated setting 7.8, which navpy never commands); a zoom command another program sends to the camera is not in the log, so its effect is seen only as a reading change (unknown) or not at all if the reading stays the same; under CPU load a 0.5 s reading gap aborts zoom for the session; a POI the rate tracker does not track counts as a loss. Owner accepted the merge with these limits and the chosen values on 2026-11-04.
19. **Pitot packet decoding — `plugins/vehicle/ardupilot/airspeed_decode.py` and `mav_bus_reader.py`.** Reuse pinned pymavlink's generated `all` dialect only to validate/decode original AIRSPEED 295 bytes before dispatch by source system; keep `ardupilotmega`/`navlink` for other traffic and keep the dependency pin. This packet carries sensor value, identity and health/use flags together. Test `test_airspeed_decode.py` through the PC router, including corrupt/foreign packets and existing GCS/navlink regressions. Done: 100% of valid fixtures keep bytes/source/flags; 0 corrupt/foreign admissions. Verify the approved ArduPilot HIL source and SITL binary contain/send the established sensor-only sender (`GCS_MAVLINK::send_airspeed`, in `feat/sim-hil` and `feat/flight-tune`); record branch, commit and the hash of the binary that actually runs, not the branch tip: `sitl-backing` defaults to the flight-tune build (`tools/tuning/sitl_sim.py` `FIRMWARE_BINARY`), and on 2026-10-18 the flight-tune binary embeds `4c23c0a1`, the sim-hil binary `28fbab23`, both older than their branch heads. No backport or firmware change is assumed: missing provenance/sender stops approaches pending a separately reviewed scope decision.
   *Built 2026-11-04, unit level, through the PC router and a live SITL send check; merged 2026-11-04* (`plugins/vehicle/ardupilot/airspeed_decode.py`; `mav_bus_reader.py` and `mav_bus.py`; branch `claude/hitl-step19-airspeed-decode-86f350`). The bus keeps `ardupilotmega` (navlink included), which has no AIRSPEED: pymavlink hands 295 over as `MAVLink_unknown`, with its original bytes but source system 0 (so the bus fanned it out to every receiver) and no CRC check. The reader now runs every packet through one `AirspeedDecoder` (made by `MavBus`, counts `decoded` and `refused` on `MavBus.airspeed`) before heartbeats and dispatch by source system: only an unknown whose original MAVLink 2 header says 295 is decoded, with a fresh read of its own bytes by the pin's generated `all` dialect (`decode()`: marker, length against the header, CRC with AIRSPEED's CRC extra 234); the result keeps the buffer, sequence, source system/component, sensor `id` and `flags`, and goes only to the receiver attached for its own source system (`MavBusMembership.receivers_of`); the bus's fallback that hands an unattached source's packets to every receiver stays for all other traffic. A 295 that fails is refused and reaches no receiver (never passed on as an unknown); every other packet, including unknowns `all` knows (such as RADIO_RC_CHANNELS 420), keeps the shared dialect's result. No dependency or XML change; which source and flags may feed the law is step 20. Tests: `test_airspeed_decode.py` (15: 9 valid fixtures, every flag combination, both sensors, two fleet systems, a non-autopilot component, a trimmed zero tail, INT16_MAX temperature and NaN pressure, seq 255; 5 corrupt (payload, tail, CRC, header source, wrong CRC extra) and another message relabelled 295 refused; other unknowns and MAVLink 1 untouched; through `MavRouter` from a fake board into a real `MavBus` on UDP: each system's receiver gets exactly its own AIRSPEED with bytes, source and flags kept, corrupt ones reach no receiver although the router forwards them, ATTITUDE, VFR_HUD and navlink SWARM_ACK unchanged and in order, a valid AIRSPEED from an unattached system reaches no receiver while that system's ATTITUDE still reaches both); mutations that skip the decoder or route AIRSPEED through the fallback fail the router tests. `pymavlink.dialects.v20.all` joins the reviewed imports in `tests/guards/runtime_time_lint.py`; the reader's UDP check is inline (SOLID field limit). Results: 100 % of valid fixtures kept, 0 corrupt or foreign admissions. Sender provenance (`.logs/hitl-step19/provenance.md`; source at the embedded commit and the head both have `GCS_MAVLINK::send_airspeed` and MSG_AIRSPEED in ArduPlane's RAW_SENSORS stream, generated CRC extra 234; builds clean): the pc-sitl runner's default `feat/sim-hil` binary (head `9a8c7d66b2`) embeds and reports `28fbab23`, sha256 `3c2facbb…`; `sitl-backing`'s default `feat/flight-tune` binary (head `064bc3b243`) embeds and reports `4c23c0a1`, sha256 `30e9502c…`; both have the `send_airspeed` symbol. Live: each binary ran SITL's plane model for 20 s with AIRSPEED requested at 50,000 microseconds; the bytes, parsed as the bus does, gave 348 AIRSPEED each, 0 refused, all bytes kept, source 1/1, sensor 0, flags UNHEALTHY at boot then USING. Codex review: round 1 found 1 point (P2: a valid AIRSPEED from an unattached system reached every receiver through the fallback), verified and fixed; round 2 found nothing open; logs in `.logs/hitl-step19/review`. My values: none in the code (the live check's 20 s and SITL instance 37 are test choices). Limits: the decoder, like the rest of the bus, does not check MAVLink 2 signatures (navpy sets up no signing); the router still parses with `ardupilotmega`, so its `board_seq_gaps` file AIRSPEED under source 0/0 and count false gaps for the autopilot once AIRSPEED streams (step 20's 20 Hz claim), a follow-up; navpy's own packet-loss count now sees AIRSPEED under the autopilot, which removes the same false gaps there; the live check used SITL's plane model, not the HIL model, so pitot values in HIL come from JSBSim through the bridge and are step 20's to check. Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-04.
20. **Eligible airspeed — `plugins/vehicle/ardupilot/flight_telemetry.py`, `inbound_router.py` and a HITL law-input guard.** Claim AIRSPEED at 20 Hz (50,000 microseconds) through existing router stream claims; require accepted acknowledgment and ≥18 Hz over 60 healthy seconds. Admit only fresh, healthy, in-use packets from the own autopilot's selected sensor, plus fresh SYS_STATUS present/enabled/healthy; never fall back to VFR_HUD (flight-summary telemetry). Pin effective `ARSPD_TYPE`/`ARSPD_USE`, sensor and firmware mapping before arming. Unknown/stale/changed source revokes and fails the trial without readmission. Test `test_approach_airspeed.py` in pinned SITL for healthy→unhealthy, disabled/not-used, stale status and plausible fallback HUD values. Done: all 4 source states handled, 0 unproven/fallback law inputs or post-revoke commands; flight refuses this simulated-source profile.
   *Built 2026-11-05, unit level and live pc-sitl SITL checks; merged 2026-11-05* (`plugins/vehicle/ardupilot/airspeed_profile.py`, `airspeed_eligibility.py`, `airspeed_attach.py`; `app/approach/law_airspeed.py`; `app/perception/integration_timing_input.with_law_airspeed`; `inbound_router.py` own-autopilot types; `tools/tuning/approach_airspeed.py`, `approach_verdict.py`, `approach.py`; branch `claude/hitl-step20-eligible-airspeed-22bea0`). Design: on the integration (HITL) path navpy attaches one `LawAirspeed` per vehicle: it claims AIRSPEED 295 at 50,000 microseconds with SET_MESSAGE_INTERVAL through the router (repacked in navpy's name, so the board acknowledges navpy), waits for the accepted COMMAND_ACK, and reads the profile's pins (`ARSPD_TYPE`, `ARSPD_USE`, `ARSPD_PRIMARY`) fresh from the board. `AirspeedEligibility` opens its 60 s window only after an accepted ack and matching pins; it admits once that window holds ≥18 Hz of packets from the own autopilot (system and component 1) for the pinned sensor with `USING` set, `UNHEALTHY` clear and a finite value, each with SYS_STATUS no older than 0.5 s showing differential pressure present, enabled and healthy (a packet without it restarts the window). A fault before admission restarts the window; after admission any fault (unhealthy, not used, invalid value, another sensor in use, stale packet or status, bad status, a changed pin) revokes for good, with its reason logged (`NAVPY_AIRSPEED_V1` records). The law reads airspeed only through `LawAirspeed.read` (None unless admitted and fresh at read time); `AirspeedGuardedActuator` wraps the navigation actuator and takes the link's send lock first (`VehicleMav.exclusive_send`, re-entrant; lock order send, then source), reads the clock, and sends only inside `AirspeedEligibility.read(now, send)`, which holds the source's lock through the check and the send: a wait for another sender cannot age the check, and a revoke on the bus thread lands wholly before (nothing sent) or after it: 0 commands after a revoke; VFR_HUD is never read for the law. The router passes AIRSPEED and SYS_STATUS only from the own autopilot. The profile exists only for pc-sitl (`SIMULATED_PROFILES`), so `flight`, `qualification` and `hil` modes refuse at attach. The runner pins the board before takeoff (`approach_airspeed.pin_board`: the three parameters, the launched binary's sha256 in `FIRMWARE_PINS` and AUTOPILOT_VERSION's reported commit) and after the run fails the trial on a revoke or a source never admitted (`approach_airspeed.outcome`, in `approach_verdict.verdict`, split out of `approach.py` for its 300-line limit). HIL airspeed chain (checked in code, not live): JSBSim calibrated airspeed → the bridge's `ind_airspeed` (cm/s, uint16) → SIM_HIL.cpp `airspeed = s.ind_airspeed` → SITL's analog pitot pin (sitl_airspeed.cpp, noise and fail options) → `AP_Airspeed_analog` with the boot offset and `ARSPD_RATIO` → AIRSPEED, the value the law sees; so pc-sitl's effective `ARSPD_TYPE` is ArduPlane's SITL default 2 (no `hitl_board.parm` there; that file's type 100 is for serial boards) and `ARSPD_USE` 1 comes from `models/plane.parm`. Tests: `test_approach_airspeed.py` (45 unit: admission, pitot without fresh status never counted, below 18 Hz, window only after ack and pins, refused or missing ack, pin mismatch and unreadable, faults before admission, gaps, each revoke reason, stale status at read and on arrival, another sensor, a changed pin, 0 guarded writes after a revoke, the lock held through the write, a write that waited 0.3 s for the send lock refused, the send lock re-entrant, a 25 m/s VFR_HUD never read, flight-mode refusals, record round trip, attach, router own-autopilot filter; plus 1 live), `test_approach_airspeed_runner.py` (16: pins pass, each refusal, pins before takeoff, outcomes). Live (2026-11-05, pinned sim-hil binary sha256 `3c2facbb…`, SITL plane model, instance 37, through the PC router, `.logs/hitl-step20/live`): healthy→unhealthy (offset 0, no skip-cal) admitted after 60.0 s at 20.0 Hz, revoked `sensor_unhealthy`; `ARSPD_USE` 0 admitted, revoked `pin_changed` (the PARAM_VALUE arrived before an unflagged packet); stale status (SYS_STATUS withheld 1 s navpy-side) admitted, revoked `status_stale` at 0.508 s; 0 law reads after each revoke; VFR_HUD 0.0 m/s with the law read None while pending. Rerun on the final code (after the review fixes): the same three revokes, 0 reads after each, pytest pass in 192 s. `pin_board` on the same SITL (`.logs/hitl-step20/pins`) read 2/1/0 and commit `28fbab23` and passed; a wrong sha256 was refused. Codex review: round 1 found 3 points: P1 a revoke could fall between the guard's check and its send (verified; fixed: the send runs inside `read` under the source's lock), P2 the window counted pitot packets without SYS_STATUS (verified; fixed), P2 any accepted interval ack could stand for the claim's (verified in principle; not reachable in this wiring, see Limits); both new tests fail on the round-1 code; round 2 confirmed those fixes and the ack limit, and found 1 point: P2 the freshness check ran before the send's wait for the link's send lock (verified: `goto` holds it across `set_mode`), fixed by taking the send lock first (`exclusive_send`, VehicleMav facade ratchet 88 → 89); round 3 found nothing material; logs in `.logs/hitl-step20/review`. Results: all 4 source states handled, 0 unproven or fallback law inputs, 0 post-revoke reads or commands; flight modes refuse the simulated profile. My values: `AIRSPEED_MAX_AGE_S` 0.2 (4 claimed periods), `STATUS_MAX_AGE_S` 0.5 (5 periods of the runner's 10 Hz streams), `CLAIM_ACK_TIMEOUT_S` 3.0, `PARAM_READ_TIMEOUT_S` 2.0, pc-sitl profile type 2 / use 1 / sensor 0, `FIRMWARE_PINS` sha256 `3c2facbb…` → `28fbab23`, `VERSION_TIMEOUT_S` 5.0; live test choices: instance 37, a 20 Hz law reader, a 1 s status hold. Limits: MAVLink's COMMAND_ACK names no message id, so any SET_MESSAGE_INTERVAL ack to navpy during the wait counts as the claim's; this holds only because the board addresses each ack to its sender and the router passes it only to that client (`mav_router_policy.py` routing), and navpy's other interval requests (pose streams, `real_detector_startup`) go out at vision start, after `attach`'s blocking wait; a later refusal still revokes (fail closed); a revoke during NAV ends as `source_command_stale`, not a named airspeed reason; SYS_STATUS rides the runner's 10 Hz streams, not a claim; only the integration path is guarded (legacy laws read airspeed unguarded); live stale status was simulated navpy-side, because a router client cannot stop another client's stream; the live not-used revoke came via `pin_changed`; at rest VFR_HUD read 0, so the plausible-HUD case is unit level only; the live checks used SITL's plane model, not the HIL bridge chain, and flew no approach; type 2 is also a real analog pitot's type, so only the firmware pin marks the source as simulated; the router's false `board_seq_gaps` with AIRSPEED (todo 276), the timing reader's unknown zoom events (todo 278) and two flaky step 19 router tests (todo 277) are logged, not fixed; `test_runtime_time_lint`'s host-member check fails on `main` too (shared venv). Owner accepted the merge with these limits and the chosen values on 2026-11-05.
21. **Search sweep — `core/command/gimbal/gimbal_search.py` and `gimbal_visual_tracking.py`.** Build a profile-defined, dock-independent scan only while nothing is detected; reuse the existing fenced SIYI rate actuator. Test `test_approach_search.py` for own detection, vision loss, revoke and scenario permutations. Done: 10/10 seeded replays stop search on detection/revoke, 0 truth/heading reads; the stage-3 envelope also guards this writer.
   *Built 2026-11-05, unit level and seeded closed-loop replays; merged 2026-11-05* (`core/command/gimbal/gimbal_search.py`, `gimbal_search_pattern.py`; the search tick in `gimbal_visual_tracking.py`; `app/perception/gimbal_search_profile.py`; `integration_timing_input.with_gimbal_revoke`; the `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` profile's `gimbal.search`; branch `claude/hitl-step21-search-sweep-021937`). Design: `GimbalSearchPattern` reads the profile's yaw ends, pitch rows, rate, tolerance, approach time and three timing limits (refused when a value is missing, not a number, not finite, outside the ZR10's ±135° yaw or -90..25° pitch, a repeated row, or a tolerance of half the yaw span or more) and builds a raster of readback-frame targets (each row end to end, rows down and back up). `GimbalSearch` steers the newest SIYI attitude readback (gimbal capture history, receipt on the capture clock) toward the current target with rate commands (rate capped at `rate_dps`, slowed over the last `approach_s`, at least one SIYI unit outside the tolerance) through the mount's gimbal actuator, the same fenced SIYI writer as rate tracking. It reads no dock, aircraft pose, heading, altitude or ground speed, so the scan is the same in every scenario; in FOLLOW the readback is body-relative joint angles, so no compass enters. The tracking sink ticks it at the tracking rate before its own update (`TrackingNavigationSink.update(pois, new_frame)`, facet `search_tick`), under the gimbal command gate; it steers only on new frames. The scan moves only while no session tracks (none, armed and never tracked, or back to search after a loss recentre), no geo session points, the final-approach zoom is not frozen and nothing is detected: a fresh detection this tick or within `clear_s` stops it at once (one zero rate, only when its own last command moved the gimbal), as do frames older than `frame_max_age_s` and readback older than `readback_max_age_s`, missing or not finite. A detection or geo session start yields first (`yield_to`, inside the gate, before its LOCK). Each (re)start commands the widest zoom step (step 18's loss policy) and moves only once that went out, then selects FOLLOW; a failed start is retried every `START_RETRY_S`. A scan in an armed session marks it recentred, so the first acquisition restores LOCK through the existing re-acquire path. Revoke is the SIYI command fence's `closed`: it latches and the scan writes nothing more; the fence's own stop covers the gimbal. The scan is built only beside integration timing; a profile search without a fence is refused. Tests: `test_approach_search.py` (55: pattern rows and 15 refusals, the hitl profile resolves and the flight profile has none, start in FOLLOW at the widest step, arrival, slowing, the minimum unit, own detection stops at once and resumes only after `clear_s`, coast ticks steer nothing, stale frames, 4 readback faults, 3 blocking sessions, revoke latches with 0 writes, a failed write resends zero, failed zoom and FOLLOW starts, detection and geo session starts stop the scan before LOCK, an armed session's first acquisition restores LOCK, the sink's tick order, no profile no tick, the real SIYI writer path (`SiyiSdkSession` with a fenced SDK: FOLLOW then 0x07 frames, nothing after `fence.close()`), an AST check that the search modules name no pose, heading, dock or truth; 10 seeded replays) and the sink fake's new `search_tick` in `test_detector_navigation_tick.py`. Seeded replays (seeds 2101-2110, `.logs/hitl-step21/seeded_replays.json`): step 13's lane (80 m, 24 m/s), the dock 900 m ahead with lateral offsets 0 to ±150 m plus `draw_dock`'s seeded 30 m offset, navpy's `GimbalAngularPlant` behind 20 ms command and 40 ms readback latency and a modelled command fence, a truth-side projection detector at the widest step's intrinsics (36 px minimum, 10 % seeded misses), the real `GimbalNavigation`, rate tracker and tracking sink; half the sessions armed before any detection; a 4 s blind spell after tracking; revoke 3.0-3.8 s into the loss (even seeds) or 2 s or more after it (odd). Results: 10/10 scanned before the first detection (24.8-30.0 s), stopped on detection (the scan's zero is the first rate sent on the stop tick; with no session yet the plant held still for the 14-15 ticks until tracking started), never moved on a tick with a fresh detection, tracked in LOCK, returned to search in the loss and was scanning at revoke, then 0 search writes and 0 plant motion; no seed re-acquired after the loss (the dock had passed under the row); 0 truth or heading reads (a strict mount fails on any other attribute). A first pattern (rows -20/-40, ±60°, 20 °/s) missed the dock in the lane: its 14 s cycle revisits a bearing slower than the ~6.5 s the dock stays inside the detection band, so the profile now sweeps one row. Guards: GimbalNavigation's facade allowance 20 → 21 for `search_tick` (a ratchet, `solid_architecture_guard.py`); `with_gimbal_revoke` takes the fence so `build_vision_controller` stays within 150 lines. Codex review: round 1 found 3 points: P2 an armed session's scan left tracking in FOLLOW (verified; fixed as above), P2 a failed widest-zoom command was never retried while the scan moved (verified; fixed), P2 the replays checked the scan's flag and counter, not the plant (verified; the replays now model the fence on the plant and check its command); the new tests fail on the round-1 code (7 of 55); round 2 confirmed those fixes and found 1 point: P2 the plant-stop check was vacuous for the five early-armed replays (verified; fixed: each stop tick's rates must start with the scan's zero, the window must count ticks; a scan that stops without its zero fails 10/10 replays); round 3 found nothing material; logs in `.logs/hitl-step21/review`. My values (profile `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded`): yaw ±45°, one row at -17° (at 80 m and the widest step it sees about 161-600 m ahead, detectable to about 318 m), 30 °/s (a ±45° sweep takes 3 s), tolerance 1.0°, approach 0.5 s, readback age 0.5 s, frame age 1.0 s, clear 2.0 s; in code `MIN_COMMAND_UNITS` 1 (one SIYI percent), `START_RETRY_S` 1.0; no dwell. Limits: the stage-3 bench envelope (stage 3 step 1, `bench_envelope.py`) is not built yet; the scan has no writer but the mount's actuator, so an envelope on that writer will guard it, but today only the command fence does; a crashed navpy sends no stop (step 17's limit, stage 3's power cut); coverage depends on the lane: one row at -17° suits step 13's lane at 80 m and 24 m/s, not other heights or speeds, and a dock that passes under the row during a loss is not found again (all 10 replays); revoke during tracking is not in the replays (the fence's own tests cover what it drops); motion blur at 30 °/s (about 17 px at a 1/100 s exposure) may hurt ChArUco detection on real video and is not tested; no live SITL or HITL run of the scan (replays only); `tests/guards/test_runtime_time_lint.py::test_reviewed_modules_host_members_dynamic_imports_and_held_modules` fails in the shared `.venv` (OpenCV 5.0.0, fixed source text, not this change). Owner accepted the merge with these limits and the chosen values on 2026-11-05.
22. **Own-detection orbit centre — `plugins/detection/charuco/board_pose.py` and `core/skills/geo_approach/self_detected_approach.py`.** Build OpenCV ChArUco corner/board matching plus `solvePnP` (pose from known board geometry); use calibrated optics and camera position/rotation at estimated capture time. Replace the height-zero ground-ray centre only before NAV. Keep metric pose and any acquisition geolocation out of the command-law record; reject ambiguous/bad fits. Test `test_board_pose_orbit.py` on projected corners, delayed moving-camera frames and isolation mutations. Done: synthetic centre error ≤0.5 m, reprojection p95 ≤2 pixels, 0 metric-pose reads by the NAV law. This is a new build, not existing functionality.
   *Built 2026-11-05, unit level only; no live run; merged 2026-11-05* (`plugins/detection/charuco/board_pose.py`; `core/ports/board_pose.py`; `BoardCorners` on `Detection`; `core/perception/pipeline/real_board_pose.py`, `capture_position.py`, `attitude_sample_pose.py` (moved out of `real_frame_association.py` for the 300-line guard); `capture_location` on `RealFrameAssociation`; `board_pose` and `capture_camera_location` on `DetectionGeoDiagnostics`; `GeoRefCalc.camera_point_to_ned`; `core/skills/geo_approach/board_orbit_centre.py`; `self_detected_approach.py`; NED velocity on `position_sample`; the `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` profile's `detector.charuco` (400 mm squares, 296 mm markers, the scene board in `hitl_unreal_camera.md`); branch `claude/hitl-step22-orbit-centre-358c04`). Design: the ChArUco detector keeps the corners it found (ids and raw pixels) on its detection. On the frame that found them, `TrackBoardPose` gives them to the track that frame updated (box IoU ≥ 0.5, same class, `missed == 0`) and `CharucoBoardPoseSolver` fits the known board with that frame's captured intrinsics and distortion: object points in metres centred on the board, `solvePnPGeneric` with planar IPPE (both mirror solutions), Levenberg-Marquardt refinement of the better one; the translation is the board centre in the camera frame. A fit is refused (`BoardPoseRejection`, reason kept, no metric value) for fewer than 6 corners, repeated or unknown ids, corners in one row or column, non-finite input, a failed solve, a centre behind the camera, a board seen from behind, reprojection p95 over 2 px, a mirror solution within 1.5× of the best error whose centre is more than 0.25 m away, or a centre whose largest one-sigma axis from the fit covariance (J^T J with at least 0.5 px of noise) exceeds 0.3 m. The camera position at capture is the newest GLOBAL_POSITION_INT fix moved along its own NED velocity from its `time_boot_ms` to the capture instant's boot time (step 11's bracketed ATTITUDE interpolation), so link delay does not enter; a shift over 0.5 s, a frame without a bracketed capture, or a non-finite value gives none. `board_orbit_centre` (geo-approach skill) turns the camera-frame centre through the capture-time gimbal and aircraft attitude (the existing camera→gimbal→aircraft→NED chain) and the capture-time camera location into a geodetic point, returned home-relative (minus the home altitude) like the ground ray, so the orbit planner's every path compares it with the aircraft's home-relative position; it needs a frame-atomic pose and an absolute home. Before NAV, `SelfDetectedApproach` centres the waiting orbit there under the vision law; a refused fit (reason logged) and a detector without board poses (YOLO) keep the height-zero ground ray as before. Nothing is stored in `navigation_poi_location` or any NAV input. Tests: `test_board_pose_orbit.py` (36: projected corners with distortion and 0.45 px noise at 100 m/10×, 60 m/5× and 25 m/3×, 40 seeds each; noise-free exact recovery; 188 m/10× refused as uncertain; 9 refusals; an ambiguous rival refused and a same-centre rival accepted; a delayed frame from a camera at 24 m/s with the newest fix 0.15 s after capture, plus mutations: the newest fix instead (error ≈ 3.6 m) and the newest attitude after a 6° roll both fail the limit; fix-shift and non-finite refusals; the real `FrameAssociationBuilder` places the camera at the bracketed capture boot time and leaves none without a bracket; centre refusals, including no absolute home; the centre is home-relative and its height below the aircraft matches the truth; the detector's corners match a rendered board within 1 px; track matching; the waiting orbit on the board centre, the refused-fit fallback and no board pose), `tests/guards/test_board_pose_isolation.py` (5: only the 14 acquisition-chain modules may name the metric pose, capture location or their modules, none in `core/approach`, `plugins/laws` or `core/command`; the command record has no such field; poisoned board pose and capture location leave `FinalApproachVisionFrame` unchanged; poisoned solver results and capture location fed upstream through the real `FrameAssociationBuilder` and `DetectedObjectMapper` leave the POI's pixel observation and the command frame equal to a run without them; a planted `poi.geo.board_pose` read in `frame_projection.py` fails the guard). Results: centre error p95 ≤ 0.36 m and reprojection p95 ≤ 2 px in every case of the accepted range; 0 metric-pose reads by the NAV law (AST guard and poisoning). Measured corner noise (`.logs/hitl-step22/corner_noise.txt`: navpy's ChArUco detector on rendered boards, 0.7 px blur, 2 grey levels of noise, JPEG 80): RMS 0.46 px at 22 px squares, 0.36-0.58 px over 16-30 px. Range sweep at that noise (`.logs/hitl-step22/centre_sweep.txt`, 200 draws): centre error p95 0.34 m at 100 m and 0.54 m at 120 m slant at 10×, 1.27 m at 188 m; 0.45 m at 80 m and 0.68 m at 100 m at 5×. Guards: `RealFrameAssociation` passive-record allowance 18 → 19 for `capture_location` (a ratchet). My values: `MIN_POSE_CORNERS` 6, `CORNER_NOISE_FLOOR_PX` 0.5, `MAX_CENTRE_SIGMA_M` 0.3 (the sweep's p95 was about 1.5 σ), `AMBIGUITY_ERROR_RATIO` 1.5, `AMBIGUOUS_CENTRE_GAP_M` 0.25, `BOARD_TRACK_MIN_IOU` 0.5, `MAX_CAPTURE_POSITION_SHIFT_S` 0.5; from the plan: `MAX_REPROJECTION_P95_PX` 2.0. Limits: at step 23's start geometry (170 m radius, 80 m above, about 188 m slant, 10×) the 0.3 m sigma gate refuses every fit, so the orbit there keeps the height-zero ray; the 0.5 m done limit holds out to about 115 m slant at 10×. Owner ruling (2026-11-05, relayed by the parent chat): keep the 0.5 m limit and the 0.3 m covariance gate, so at step 23's start geometry (about 188 m slant at 10×) every fit is refused and the orbit uses the height-zero ground ray there; the corner noise comes from a rendered-board proxy, not decoded Unreal video; no live SITL or HITL run; only `prepare` (the first frame of a POI) places the orbit, so a refused first fit is not retried until the next start; the capture position needs the GLOBAL_POSITION_INT velocity to hold over the shift (no acceleration term); the board pose assumes the board's printed corner geometry (profile `detector.charuco`) matches the scene. Review: Codex round 1 (`.logs/hitl-step22/review/round1.txt`) found no P1 and two P2s, both verified and fixed: (1) the board centre was absolute while the ground ray and the aircraft position given to the planner are home-relative, so the planner's fallback path (`peer_offset` `alt_diff`) mixed the two; the centre is now home-relative; (2) the poisoning test acted only after the POI was built; an upstream test through the real association and mapper now covers producers. Its own pytest could not start in the read-only sandbox, so OpenCV 4.x was not run by anyone (the venv has 5.0.0). Owner accepted the merge with these limits on 2026-11-05, with one CI failure outside this step (`test_serial_success_check.py::test_wait_for_success`, a wall-clock timing flake, not a race; fixed in PR 83).

23. **Feasible orbit — `core/skills/acquire/approach_planner.py`, `peer_orbit_plan.py` and `tools/hitl/scenario_checks.py`.** Build an explicit HITL-only radius setting instead of silently inheriting the 500 m floor; start evaluation at 170 m radius/80 m above board/10×, not an automatic approval. Before each series check turn radius v²/(g tan 35°), board-square pixels against the configured reader limit, both-axis containment and recovery floor using model speed/optics. Test `test_approach_geometry.py` with feasible/impossible cases and unchanged non-HITL defaults. Done: 100% of failed checks refuse; 0 truth geometry reaches product acquisition. The owner approves this HITL setting; changing product defaults needs separate approval.
   *Built 2026-11-05, unit level only; no live run; merged 2026-11-06* (`core/skills/acquire/orbit_radius_setting.py`; `approach_planner.py`; a note in `peer_orbit_plan.py`; the `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` profile's `final_approach_orbit.radius_m`; `board_points_m` on `CharucoBoardPoseSolver`; `tools/hitl/scenario_checks.py` (the checks and CLI), `orbit_plan.py` (inputs), `orbit_centre.py` (centre prediction); `tools/tuning/approach_orbit.py` and `approach.py` (new required `--board-height-m`); branch `claude/hitl-step23-feasible-orbit-5412ef`). Design: a vision profile may set `final_approach_orbit: {radius_m}`; it replaces only the 500 m floor of `r_nav_min` (the turn and descent terms still apply on top, and the planner warns once when they raise it). A malformed setting is refused when the planner is built. Only the HITL profile sets it; every other profile keeps 500 m. The pre-series check reads the profile radius and measured zoom optics, the scenario's public lane and floor, the run's merged parameters, the model input's airspeed band and the board's design height, never a seed or dock pose. It checks: `model_speed` (the commanded airspeed inside the model band), `turn_radius` (r ≥ 2 v²/(g tan φ), φ the 35° NAV cap or the aircraft's `ROLL_LIMIT_DEG` when smaller, as the planner reads it), `descent` (r ≥ H / tan(0.65 |PTCH_LIM_MIN_DEG|), H the lane over the orbit centre: the board for an accepted pose, else the ground ray's height zero, as the planner sizes it), `reader` (squares along the line of sight at the farthest slant ≥ the reader limit), `containment` (the board box keeps the zoom-in margin on both axes at the nearest slant, and the next narrower step fails it at the farthest) and `recovery_floor` (lane above the floor, board ≥ the pull-out allowance above it). The orbit centre error is step 22's 0.5 m if the real solver accepts every seeded noisy fit at the reading slant; otherwise it is the height-zero ground ray from the first detection, D · board height / h, with D the reading slant's horizontal reach (never less than r). The approach runner runs the check before the record or run folder exists and refuses the run on any failed check or unreadable input. Owner rulings (2026-11-05): radius 300 m ("at least 300m far": 170 m leaves no room for the approach); step 22's 0.3 m covariance gate stays; seeing the net as one object far out is a separate later step (a whole-object dock detector); the check is wired into the runner now, knowing every run is refused until then. Result: at 300 m every series is refused on the reader: 3.96-8.0 px per square against 11.5 px at 80-160 m above a 16 m board, and at 160 m above also on descent (`.logs/hitl-step23/orbit_300m.json`). With the 0.3 m gate the pose is refused at the reading slant, and ChArUco at 10× is feasible only in a narrow window (for example 178.6-187.6 m at 90 m above a 10 m board with a 1 m floor). The series default floor (30 m) is above a 16-20 m board, so `recovery_floor` also refuses it. Tests: `test_approach_geometry.py` (58: non-HITL defaults unchanged; HITL radius 300 m; turn term raising it with one warning; malformed settings refused; a feasible plan and 13 failing cases; unusable inputs fail every check; the solver accepting at 100 m and refusing at the reading slant; Codex's 46.89 m case; the descent verdict matching whether the real planner flies the set radius, including Codex's 189.28 m case; a 20° `ROLL_LIMIT_DEG` refused as the planner would fly 322.64 m; the CLI refusing the 300 m profile, unreadable inputs and failing series; the check never opens the scenario's private files (poisoned `open`); no `src/navpy` file imports `tools` or names the check's inputs), and `test_approach_runner.py` (3 new: an infeasible orbit refuses the run before its folder exists; a feasible one is kept in the run; a missing `PTCH_LIM_MIN_DEG` refuses; `ROLL_LIMIT_DEG` is required the same way). Plan values: reader 11.5 px per 40 cm square, pull-out allowance 9 m, the NAV bank cap 35°, `r_nav_min`'s κ 2 and η 0.65. My values: `POSE_DRAWS` 40, `POSE_SEED` 23, `POSE_YAWS_DEG` (0, 45, 90); airspeed = max(`PASS_AIRSPEED_M_S`, `AIRSPEED_CRUISE`); the planner's warning above 0.5 m; the board box centred and sized from the board diagonal; test plans at a 180 m circle over a 10 m board with a 1 m floor (the feasible window under the ground ray). Limits: no wind (ground-speed turn radius); flat terrain at home height; the box is centred, not offset by the centre error; the corner noise is step 22's 0.5 px floor, not decoded Unreal video; the board height is the operator's design value; the airspeed is the commanded worst case, while the planner reads telemetry airspeed at plan time; the accepted-pose branch is a prediction from synthetic full-corner views, not the run's own verdict. Review: Codex round 1 (`.logs/hitl-step23/review/round1.txt`) found a P1 and a P2, both verified and fixed: (1) a failed check only set the CLI's exit code, and nothing stopped a run; the runner now refuses; (2) the ground-ray error used the radius, not the first detection's distance (46.89 m, not 37.78 m, at 170 m/90 m/20 m board, which also fails the reader); fixed and tested. Round 2 (`round2.txt`) found one P2, verified and fixed: the descent check used lane minus board height, but with the pose refused the planner sizes the descent from the whole lane over the ground ray (189.28 m, not 160.71 m, at 170 m/106 m lane/16 m board). Round 3 (`round3.txt`) found one P2, verified and fixed: the turn check used only the 35° cap, but the planner reads `ROLL_LIMIT_DEG` (at 20° it flies 322.64 m, not 180 m). It also noted, as limits, that the planner uses telemetry airspeed at plan time and that the accepted-pose branch is a prediction from synthetic corners. Round 4 (`round4.txt`, on the merged branch) found no P1 or P2, and nothing in this record that the code does not do. Codex's own pytest could not start in its read-only sandbox, so the tests were run here only. Owner accepted the merge with these limits and the chosen values on 2026-11-06.
23b. **Whole-dock detector, phase A — `plugins/detection/rfdetr` (new), the HITL profile, `tools/hitl/scenario_checks.py`, `tools/hitl/scenario_public.py`, the Unreal scene.** (`hitl_dock_detector.md` v2.2, approved by the owner 2026-11-08.) Put a Ford car on the ground at the dock POI in the Unreal scene, with no other car near it; run RF-DETR-L (COCO weights), class `car` mapped to `dock`, in `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` with the acquisition and ambiguity gates; orbit on the ground ray; replace the reader check with the detector check; release the recovery floor at the operator's confirm. Test `test_approach_geometry.py` (detector check feasible/impossible, D beyond r, dock on the ground), detector-gate tests (two tracks, short track, class mapping), floor-release tests and the isolation guards with the section 4.2 mutations. Done: the section 6 measurements recorded; the check passes at 300 m with measured values; 0 false confirms in the declared exposure; 0 box-size, centre, heading, position or altitude reads on NAV command and completion paths.
   *Built 2026-11-09, unit level, a real-video proxy measurement and RF-DETR-L on this PC's GPU only; no Unreal car, no live run; not merged* (`plugins/detection/rfdetr/` (`rfdetr_codec.py`, `rfdetr_tiling.py`, `rfdetr_runtime.py`, `rfdetr_detector.py`, `rfdetr_settings.py`); `--detector-backend rfdetr` in `core/kernel/config/detector_backend.py` and `vision_args.py`; `app/perception/rfdetr_profile.py`, `real_detector_factory.py`, `real_detector_model_builder.py`, `RealDetectorConfig.acquisition`, `RealDetectorModels.acquisition`; `core/perception/tracking/dock_acquisition_gate.py` in `real_tracking_batch.py`, the search tick in `real_tracking.py` and the reset in `real_detector_controls.py`; `app/flow/confirm_record.py`, `released`/`entered` on `AcquisitionSequence` (which now places the orbit from the task start's frame), `on_release` on `ConfirmedPoiRelease`, `entered` on `NavEntryPorts`; `tools/tuning/flight_floor.py` `HeightFloor.release`, `approach_quiesce.observe`, `approach.py` (`--detector-model` replaces `--board-height-m`), `approach_chain.py`, `tools/hitl/approach_run_checks.py`; `tools/hitl/scenario_public.py` and `scenario_service.py` (schema 2, `dock_size`), `orbit_plan.py`, `orbit_centre.py`, `scenario_checks.py`; `tools/hitl/dock_detector_scale.py`; the profile's `detector.conf`, `rfdetr` and `acquisition` blocks (its `charuco` block removed); a layer exception row beside ChArUco's in `docs/design/step1/exceptions.tsv`; `rfdetr` in the runtime lint's reviewed modules; branch `claude/eager-ishizaka-7fefbb`). Design: the rfdetr backend loads RF-DETR-L weights (`--detector-model-path`, private, outside git) through pip `rfdetr` 1.12.0's own torch model (eval mode, no gradients, FP16 autocast on CUDA like gimbal-controller's live FP16 engine; a numeric-string device is a CUDA index) and does gimbal-controller's pre- and post-processing itself (the frame squashed to the 704 px square, bilinear without antialias, RGB, ImageNet-normalised; the sigmoid of all 300 x 91 query/class pairs, the 300 best by score then index, floor 0.05, no NMS; COCO category ids mapped to 0-79). The profile keeps COCO `car` only, maps it to `dock` (class 0) and drops rows under `detector.conf` 0.45. One detector, no fallback: a frame it misses has no detection. Its input is `squash` (one 704 px square) or `tiles` (a fixed grid of native 704 px tiles, six on 1920 x 1080, normalised in one pass, one batch; a box cut by an inner tile edge yields to an uncut one in the next tile, two cut boxes never drop each other, overlaps merge to the best score); the profile uses `tiles`. The crop after lock is not built. The acquisition gate sits after the identity layer: until one `dock` track, confirmed, in view in every frame, at or above the confidence, with its smaller box side at least `min_box_px` in the detector's own input pixels, has lasted `min_lock_s` on the tracking clock, no track passes on, so no POI exists and nothing locks or starts a task; while it holds a dock back it reports a candidate, and the search sweep holds still on it as on a detection (step 21). Once acquired only that identity passes, whatever its box, also when it returns after a gap; more than one dock track in view holds every track back, before and after acquisition (fail closed: the lock, orbit, confirm and NAV lose their frames); another dock in view never replaces the acquired identity: the acquisition ends only when that identity's ownership is released, by the detector's `stop_tracking` (which also resets the POI lock: task teardown, rejection, a long loss before the geo hand-off, a peer hand-off) or its reset (the tracker and identity restart their ids); a lock guards the gate between the tracking and navigation threads. The orbit centre is the existing height-zero ground ray (there is never a board pose), taken at the lock from the newest frame of the task that the centre-frame rule accepts (`core/skills/geo_approach/orbit_centre_frame.py`): its box, in the detector's input pixels scaled from the frame's own focal length to the widest step's, still meets `min_box_px`, and it holds what the ground ray reads (frame-atomic pose, aircraft attitude, gimbal readback, camera matrix, camera location). So the distance D_max bounds also bounds the frame used, however late the task started or the lock came; a frame the orbit start refuses is dropped and the next accepted one is tried; with none, the orbit waits. Without the whole-dock detector every frame is accepted, so step 25 still places it from the lock frame. Floor release (option A): when a task's confirm releases NAV (step 25's gates holding on that tick, skipped only while NAV is both committed (`previous`) and current: a failed entry leaves the requested NAV in `current` only and a failed exit leaves it in `previous` only, and both pass the gates again; a tick without them drops the pending release, and an entry that completes more than the lock's own frame gap (`lock_max_frame_gap_s`, 0.5 s) after the release counts nothing, so a held or retried entry leaves the floor armed and a descent then trips it: fail closed) and a NAV entry then completes (zoom frozen, GUIDED requested, navigation and dock contact armed; a failed entry releases nothing), navpy prints `NAVPY_CONFIRM_V1 {"task": N}` once per task; the runner's `observe` hands it to `HeightFloor.release`, which stops the floor for the rest of the run; a breach before it still stands; `record["height"]["released"]` keeps the reason. Stage 1 only. The scenario's public half (schema 2) and the series carry `dock_size` (kind `car`, length, width, height, placement `ground`), a scene design fact only the check reads; schema 1 series and run records are refused. The check: `check_detector` (the dock's smaller box side over 12 yaws, in input pixels, at the farthest slant r + e at least `recall_min_box_px`; the gate's `min_box_px` not below it; and that floor met at the search step at the radius or beyond, so the gate can acquire there); `containment` on the car's box at its largest yaw; `recovery_floor` (the lane above the floor; the dock no longer above it). e is the plain sum of declared bounds at D_max, the farthest distance the gate can acquire at the widest (search) step: POI height D hd / h, lane height D dh / h, depression S^2 / h, azimuth S psi, box-centre px and capture skew. Result: the owner's 300 m series passes every check: D_max 361.6 m, e 24.8 m (3.4 POI height, 4.6 lane, 6.0 depression, 6.5 azimuth, 1.9 box centre, 2.4 skew), the car 91 input px at 334 m slant at 10x. Measurements (section 6), a real-video proxy only: no Unreal car exists, so items 1-4 and 6 cannot run as designed. Item 1 proxy (`tools/hitl/dock_detector_scale.py`, `.logs/hitl-step23b/scale_check.json`): 8 frames of each of the owner's 11 real ZR10 car clips (gimbal-controller, private), shrunk 1-0.1x, every reference car of the clip's fixed reference; per-frame recall by smaller box side in input pixels, squash / tiles, FP16: 16-20 px 0.30 / 0.50, 20-24 px 0.47 / 0.61, 24-32 px 0.49 / 0.69, 32-48 px 0.77 / 0.66, 48+ px 0.79 / 0.78 (n 340-1548 per bin), under 10 px nearly 0 in both; FP32 gave the same within 0.04. Tiles find more below 32 px, as the design expected. Neither mode reaches the approved 95 % per frame at any size on this real video (crowded lots, occlusion, night; the reference's boxes counted as truth); isolated cars scored lower still on small samples. Squash boxes match rfdetr's own `predict()` on a real frame to under 2 px (test, FP16). Item 5, RF-DETR-L in eager PyTorch on this PC's RTX PRO 1000 laptop GPU, the whole call: FP32 squash p50 84-89 ms, tiles 391-419 ms; FP16 (shipped) squash p50 65 ms, p95 106 ms; tiles p50 265 ms, p95 309 ms. Items 2 (0 false confirms in 2 h), 3, 4 and 6 need the Unreal car: not measured. Tests: `test_orbit_centre_frame.py` (15: the floor at the search step, a zoomed-in frame judged at the search step, squash pixels, each missing pose input, the newest accepted frame placing the orbit, a refused frame dropped, waiting with none, the profile's rule and its wiring), `test_rfdetr_detector.py` (34: codec class mapping, floor, top-k ties, refused shapes and non-finite outputs, RGB normalisation, one-pass tiles; tiling grid, cut boxes including two cut ones, merges; squash and tiles through a fake runtime, car only as the dock, no fallback; settings refused when malformed; devices; the factory and model builder; parity with rfdetr's own boxes on a real frame, skipped without the package, weights or clips), `test_dock_acquisition_gate.py` (25: acquisition after `min_lock_s`, a short track, two tracks, a second track restarting the dwell, each ineligible box/confidence/state, input pixels, after acquisition box size and confidence ignored, a second dock holding everything back, the same identity returning small, another dock never replacing the acquired identity, `stop_tracking` releasing it, reset, the detector reset, malformed blocks, the real association processor and the sink's search hold), `test_confirm_release.py` (14, with the committed-phase cases, a failed exit, the frame-gap window and a pending release dropped: the record, once per task, only after a completed NAV entry, a failed NAV entry releasing nothing, and the real navigation graph printing it once at NAV and never without a lock), `test_floor_release.py` (4), `tests/guards/test_dock_detector_isolation.py` (9: no NAV module names the detector, gate or release; box size, orbit centre, heading, position, altitude and dock height changed or poisoned leave the command frame, the law's command, the visual-pass plan and the confirmability unchanged, also through the real mapper), `test_approach_geometry.py` (60), the step 25 orbit test, and the runner, report, records and scenario tests updated for `rfdetr`, `--detector-model` and `dock_size`. Mutations (`.logs/hitl-step23b/mutations.txt`): each of 55 fails the step's tests (box size, orbit centre, heading, position, altitude and dock height fed into the command ray, the law's airspeed or the confirm decision; each gate rule dropped or bent, including the ambiguity hold after acquisition, keeping the identity over a gap and the reset; class map, confidence, tile offsets, cut-box rules, COCO map, RGB order and the device index; the floor release, its once-per-task, pending-confirm and completed-entry rules, the orbit's start frame and the runner's hand-over; the centre-frame rule, its scaling, pose check, refused-frame drop and wiring; the committed-phase bypass and its current-NAV half, the pending drop, the release window and its wiring; another dock replacing the acquisition; `stop_tracking` keeping it; check_detector at r, the gate-vs-recall and reachability rules, D_max's box-size error, e at r and the old floor rule). Affected suites: `tests/tools`, `tests/modules/vision`, `nav`, `navigation` and `tests/guards` in the shared `.venv`: 7028 passed, 14 skipped, 4 failed, the known OpenCV 5 `test_board_detector` failures (`.logs/hitl-step23b/affected_tests.txt`); the step's tests with the parity test in the step's venv: 138 passed. Environment: `rfdetr` 1.12.0 is not a project dependency (the owner decides); it runs in its own venv, `%LOCALAPPDATA%\navpy-hitl-tools\venv-rfdetr` (Python 3.11; torch 2.11.0+cu128 and torchvision 0.26.0+cu128 from the PyTorch cu128 index, then `rfdetr==1.12.0` and `opencv-python-headless>=4.8,<5` (4.14), then `pip install -e .[test]` of this worktree; its log and freeze beside it), never the shared `.venv`. The weights are gimbal-controller's `rf-detr-large-2026.pth`. Limits: no Unreal car, scene or live run (the series still cannot run); the recall target is unmet on the proxy and unmeasured on renders; the pipeline drops a detection older than 0.25 s from capture (`FreshnessPolicy` at the 60 Hz tracking rate), and tiles take about 0.25 s of inference alone on this PC, so the profile's tiles cannot pass acquisition live here: a faster runtime (a TensorRT FP16 engine, as on orin1) or the owner's choice of squash (which cannot acquire beyond the 300 m circle at the search step, so the check would refuse) is needed; the freshness rule is not weakened; the WSL runner's Python needs `rfdetr` and the weights at a WSL path; a second car in view holds the approach back, so it waits or ends; the orbit waits while no frame meets the centre-frame rule. Review (Codex, `.logs/hitl-step23b/review/`): round 1 raised five P1 and four P2, all verified and fixed or recorded: the floor released before NAV entry completed (now after it); the gate kept its acquisition over a pipeline reset (now reset with it); a brief loss re-imposed the box floor in NAV (the identity is now kept); a second dock after acquisition was hidden (now everything is held back); two cut tile boxes dropped each other (fixed); the orbit centred on the later lock frame, not the one D_max bounds (now the task start's frame); the check accepted an unreachable gate floor (now refused); a numeric-string device (fixed); the 0.25 s observation age, tighter than the frame gap (now in the limits; FP16 and one-pass tiles brought tiles from about 0.42 s to about 0.25 s). Round 2 (`round2.txt`) judged six resolved and three partly, and raised two new P1, and the owner chose a third round; all fixed in round 3's commit: (a) a failed NAV entry leaves the phase at NAV, so a retry skips the step 25 gates and its `entered` releases the floor on the old confirmation without a fresh lock; (b) the stored task-start frame's pose is never validated, so a frame with missing attitude or a failed capture association fixes a bad or failing orbit centre; partly: another dock appearing while the acquired one is out of view ends the acquisition, so the acquired car returning small is held back; the task-start frame is not strictly the acquisition frame (a deferred start); and finding 1 above leads to (a). Round 3 (`round3.txt`) judged (b) and the frame bound resolved and raised: a held entry (recovery's LOITER hold skips the confirm path) could still consume an old pending release (now the frame-gap window); another dock could still replace an identity the POI lock still owned (now never; released only with the POI lock); and a new P1: a failed NAV exit leaving `previous` at NAV let the gates be skipped from DETECT (now the bypass needs NAV current too). The round 3 fixes are not re-reviewed: a fourth round waits for the owner's word.
24. **Camera-motion feedforward — `core/perception/pointing/gimbal_rate_tracker.py` and `gimbal_tracking_sample.py`.** Build camera-motion-compensated visual LOS rate and feedforward (predicted rate added to tracking correction), using calibrated mount and associated camera state. Reuse the tracker/actuator; the compensation does not exist yet. Test `test_approach_visual_control.py` with fixed/moving visual rays and camera motion. Done: synthetic zero-target-motion residual ≤0.1°/s, correct sign in 10 replays, 0 compass-heading or truth reads; apply the owner's gyro ruling.
   *Built 2026-11-06, unit level and synthetic closed-loop replays only; no live run; merged 2026-11-06* (`core/perception/pointing/camera_motion_state.py`, `compensated_los_rate.py`; `gimbal_tracking_sample.py`, `gimbal_rate_tracker.py`, `gimbal_rate_command.py`, `gimbal_rate_types.py`; the `gimbal.tracking.camera_feedforward` key in `app/perception/vision_tracking_profile.py`, set only by the `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` profile; branch `claude/hitl-step24-camera-feedforward-b98c27`). Design: each sample from `GimbalPoiProjector` now carries the frame's camera state, built only from a frame-atomic pinhole `PixelObservation`: its `camera_to_body` (calibrated mount and the gimbal readback associated at capture, step 11), aircraft pitch/roll and `aircraft_yaw_rate_rad_s` (body q/r gyros with pitch/roll, never heading). `CompensatedLosRate` turns the pixel ray through `camera_to_body` and pitch/roll (yaw 0, the frame projector's convention) into a heading-free level frame. Elevation there is world elevation; the azimuth turns with the aircraft, so each step adds the trapezoid gyro turn to the wrapped azimuth change, giving the azimuth relative to the first sample, never an absolute heading. The tracker's own constant-velocity estimator over these two angles gives the LOS rate; the command law adds it, in deg/s, to the existing correction (bandwidth × projected image error) before the unit clamp, preview/commit like the rest of the tracker (a failed actuation commits nothing). This assumes the gimbal's rate commands turn the camera about the world vertical, as in LOCK (the tracking session's mode); in FOLLOW the yaw feedforward would need the turn rate taken off again. No camera state (not associated, spherical, no gyro), a gap over 0.25 s or |elevation| over 80° gives zero feedforward and restarts the estimate. Off unless a profile turns it on, so every other profile and the sim keep the old law. Owner ruling (2026-11-06): the gyro turn rate is allowed in the gimbal-pointing feedforward only; the `vision_pn` law question under Owner decisions stays open. Results (`.logs/hitl-step24/evidence.txt`): fixed visual ray with the aircraft turning (8 ± 3.9 °/s), pitching ±4°, rolling 20 ± 6° and the camera wobbling ±2° yaw, ±1.5° elevation: residual 0.0006 °/s after 2 s (done ≤0.1); moving rays (±8.1 °/s azimuth, ±3 °/s elevation) match to 0.0006 °/s; constant pixels while the camera turns at 6 °/s give 6 °/s. Ten seeded replays with 0.1° readback steps, 0.3 px pixel noise and gyro noise 0.1 °/s plus a bias (σ 0.05 °/s): the sign is right on every settled frame of every replay, mean within 0.13 °/s, p95 error 0.13-0.35 °/s. Closed loop on a 170 m, 24 m/s orbit with a one-frame command delay: steady pointing error 3.23° without feedforward (design N7's (v/r)/bw 3.2°), 0.00006° with it. Mutations: dropping the gyro term fails 16 of 22 tests, flipping the elevation sign 12. Tests: `test_approach_visual_control.py` (22: the cases above, missing camera state, a long gap, steep elevation, a failed actuation, the same feedforward under heading offsets of 90°, -137° and 179°) and `test_approach_visual_control_inputs.py` (13: an AST guard that the five pointing modules name no heading, yaw read, attitude object, raw body rates, ground speed, position, altitude, range, geo or sim truth; the level frame writes yaw 0; the projector's camera state; none without an associated pinhole frame; poisoned compass yaw, geo, reference height and sim flag give the same sample; only the HITL profile turns it on; a non-boolean setting is refused); helper `camera_motion_world.py`. My values: `max_gap_s` 0.25 (the estimator's `max_step_dt`), `max_abs_elevation_deg` 80, the aircraft sequence ZYX (`UasArgs` default, the frame projector's), the estimator settings reused from the tracker's, and the test scenarios and noise levels above. Limits: synthetic only, no SITL, Unreal or ZR10 run; the real ZR10's LOCK rate frame, readback lag and its 0.1° readback steps at the real rate are not measured; the camera roll is taken as zero in the command axes (as the existing law does); near the nadir the yaw feedforward is withheld rather than solved. Review: Codex round 1 (`.logs/hitl-step24/review/round1.txt`, on commit 6b390e35) found no P1, P2 or P3; its pytest could not start in its sandbox, so the tests were run here only. Owner accepted the merge with these limits and the chosen values on 2026-11-06.
25. **Lock and confirm gates — `core/skills/confirm/confirmation_action.py`, `confirmed_poi_release.py` and acquisition composition.** Build explicit fresh-visual-lock and orbit-established gates before confirm/NAV, composing search → own detection → lock → ORBIT on the own-detected pose → confirm → NAV. Freeze numerical lock dwell, image-error and orbit tolerances in the profile during commissioning; no truth-selected transitions. Test `test_approach_acquisition.py` with each gate missing, truth poisoned throughout and compass heading poisoned at the visual-control/law boundary. Done: correct order in 10 seeded replays, 0 premature confirmations/NAV entries; invalid vision blocks both.
   *Built 2026-11-07, unit level and seeded replays through the real navigation graph only; no live run; merged 2026-11-08* (`core/skills/confirm/acquisition_gate_settings.py`, `fresh_visual_lock.py`, `orbit_established.py`, `acquisition_sequence.py`; `confirmation_action.py`, `confirmed_poi_release.py`; `core/review/confirmation_acquisition_hold.py`, the gates' state in `ConfirmationRegistryState`, `cancel` on `ConfirmationWorkerState`; `SelfDetectedApproach.prepare` now returns the circle it commanded; `app/flow/nav_acquisition_composition.py` (the ORBIT builder, moved out of `nav_task_composition.py`) and the confirmation compositions; the `siyi_zr10_hitl_encoded` profile's `final_approach_gates`; branch `claude/hitl-step25-lock-confirm-gates-db95cf`). Design: a vision profile may set `final_approach_gates: {lock_dwell_s, lock_max_image_error_deg, lock_max_frame_gap_s, orbit_radial_tolerance_m, orbit_dwell_s}`; a malformed block is refused when navigation is built, and it needs the ORBIT approach. Without it nothing changes (`OPEN_ACQUISITION`). With it, the navigation task's start (own detection; search stops on it, step 21) only marks the POI. `FreshVisualLock` holds once one track's frames (frame-atomic pinhole frames, fresh by confirm's 1.0 s rule, detection armed) kept the POI within the image error of the optical axis (the angle of the pixel's pinhole ray, frame-local) for the dwell on the frames' own timestamps. It holds on a tick only when that tick shows a good frame (a new one, or the newest good one repeated within the frame gap). A blip (missing, invalid or off-axis frames) does not lose it; it is lost when no good frame has arrived for longer than the frame gap on the decision clock, two good frames are further apart than that on their own timestamps, a frame comes from another track, or the frames' time goes back (these two on any frame, good or not; owner ruling 2026-11-07: "we should not lose but if we lost and not sure we should ask again"; the blip window is the profile's frame gap, no new value). On the first lock the ORBIT is commanded from that frame's own pose (step 22's board centre or the ground ray; never truth), always the own circle (a circle already flown, such as a peer's, is not kept). `OrbitEstablished` holds once the aircraft's stamped GPS fix (`vehicle.position_sample`; GPS is allowed before NAV) stayed within the radial tolerance of that circle (horizontal haversine distance) for the dwell on the decision clock; a fix counts only while its boot time advances, and a missing or non-finite fix, a boot-time gap or step back, no new fix for `ORBIT_MAX_OBSERVATION_GAP_S` or an observation gap that long restarts the dwell. The orbit is observed on every CONFIRM tick, with or without a frame and in every confirmation status. Confirm (local or operator request) needs both gates on this tick. Each tick the sequence publishes whether they are still held (the lock kept through a blip, the orbit established) to the confirmation lock (`ConfirmationAcquisitionHold`); under that lock the registry refuses CONFIRMED from every path (operator answer, timeout or auto-confirm worker, local confirm) while they do not hold, clearing the status so the POI is asked again, and publishing a loss before NAV withdraws a review under way (its worker cancelled, its round retired, its status cleared), so a later answer is late, and revokes a CONFIRMED the same way (fail closed: an answer can be judged against the previous tick while this tick already sees the loss), so a confirmation counts only while the gates held on every tick since it, and the POI is asked again. `ConfirmedPoiRelease` holds NAV until both hold on its own tick too. Without the gates nothing is published and every path behaves as before. Nothing in the gates reads heading, attitude, altitude, ground speed, range, dock geo or truth (AST guard). Tests: `test_approach_acquisition.py` (67: settings; the lock's dwell, error angle, each loss, an off-axis blip (repeated too) and off-axis frames past the gap, and every invalid-vision kind both as a blip and past the gap; the orbit's dwell and faults, heading and altitude ignored; the sequence (orbit waits for the lock, a refused orbit retried, a vision blackout keeps the orbit dwell, another task); composition gated and open; through the real `create_nav_test_rig` graph: no lock, no orbit, both gates (NAV at 12.3 s), invalid vision blocks both including an operator CONFIRMED, a lock lost after confirmation; truth poisoned throughout and compass heading/yaw randomised on every read give identical traces and the own centre; 10 seeded replays) and `test_approach_acquisition_review.py` (27: a cached fix never holds; a stalled stream restarts; boot-time gap and step back; a vehicle without stamped fixes never establishes; a blip keeps the review and a lost lock withdraws it; the orbit keeps its dwell through a 3 s review; an answer, the timeout path and the auto-confirm path of a withdrawn review set nothing; with the gates published as failing but the review not yet withdrawn, the operator, timeout, worker and local paths are all refused; an answer while they hold is accepted; an operator CONFIRMED in the graph while the orbit is not established is refused; a repeated frame breaks the lock 0.5 s after it arrived; an answer accepted on the previous tick's state is revoked when this tick publishes the loss, and holding again does not restore it; in the graph a blip before NAV keeps the confirmation (NAV on the next good frame) and a loss before NAV revokes it, it is asked again and flown (NAV at 15.0 s); a frame at the newest good frame's time but off the axis is not held; an invalid frame from another track (also one without a timestamp) or with time going back loses the lock; a frame first seen invalid is not held when it comes again valid at the same time; a circle already flown does not stall the own ORBIT; compass yaw poisoned (137°, -91°, 359°) on the real `FinalApproachConfirmation`, `FinalApproachFrameProjector` and `VisionNavLaw` gives the same confirmability, control ray and law command). The round-1 tests failed 9 of 12 on the first commit; the three law-boundary tests are coverage of behaviour that already held. The invalid-vision test now expects the operator CONFIRMED to be refused, so NAV comes one tick later (62.2 s) after the local confirmation. The replay's metre-to-degree offset uses `Location.distance_to`'s sphere, so a scripted radial is the measured one. Results (`.logs/hitl-step25/seeded_replays.json`): seeds 2501-2510 (search 2-6 s, the error converging from 3-8° with τ 0.5-1.5 s, 10 % missed frames, 3 % non-atomic frames, one 0.6-1.2 s blackout, the aircraft turning onto the circle from 60-150 m off with τ 8 s and 4 m noise; planner stubbed to 300 m) all keep detected < locked = orbit < established < confirmed < nav, with one confirmation and one NAV entry each at or after an independent oracle's first allowed tick (0 premature); truth and compass poisoning change no trace. Mutations (`.logs/hitl-step25/mutations.txt`): each of 26 (no lock, no release gate, no confirm gate, orbit before the lock, orbit observed only with frames, no good-frame gap check, no decision-clock gap check, time going back ignored, a blip loses the lock, another track ignored on invalid frames, the timestamp read before the track, only good frames watched, a repeated frame skips the error check, no error limit, no orbit tolerance, cached fix counts, no fix-gap check, no withdrawal, no revocation, no CONFIRMED refusal, nothing published, held-on-this-tick published instead of kept, a repeated bad frame holds, no orbit observation while CONFIRMING, peer circle kept, worker not cancelled) fails 1-20 tests. Affected suites (nav, guards, navigation, confirmation regressions, vision, swarm) after merging origin/main 8cd167c8: 5747 passed, 8 skipped (`.logs/hitl-step25/affected_tests.txt`). Guards: the board-pose isolation allow list names `nav_acquisition_composition.py` in place of `nav_task_composition.py` (the ORBIT builder moved). My values, all provisional until commissioning: the profile's `lock_dwell_s` 2.0, `lock_max_image_error_deg` 1.0, `lock_max_frame_gap_s` 0.5, `orbit_radial_tolerance_m` 25, `orbit_dwell_s` 10; `ORBIT_MAX_OBSERVATION_GAP_S` 1.0; lock freshness reuses confirm's 1.0 s; the replay seeds and scenario parameters above. Limits: no SITL, Unreal or HITL run (at step 23's 300 m every HITL series is refused); the planner is stubbed in the replays and the rig mocks the final-approach admission; the lock is on the confirm-freshness rule, not a new age limit; the 1.0 s fix gap assumes ArduPilot streams GLOBAL_POSITION_INT at 2 Hz or more (not measured on the HITL link); each loss before NAV, during a review or after a confirmation, sends the operator a new request once the gates hold again (a blip shorter than the frame gap costs no new answer; a longer gap does); the gates are sampled once per navigation tick, so an answer is judged against the most recent tick, and NAV needs them on its own tick. Review (Codex, `.logs/hitl-step25/review/`): round 1 raised cached GPS holding the orbit (P1), an operator answer after the gates stopped (P1), an existing circle stalling the own ORBIT (P2) and a compass test that missed the law boundary (P2); round 2 judged those resolved and raised the withdrawal racing the response thread (P1) and a repeated frame keeping the lock (P2); round 3 raised an answer judged on the previous tick's state (P1), fixed fail closed by revoking CONFIRMED on a loss before NAV. The owner then ruled that a short blip must not cost a new answer (the blip rule above). Round 4, on that change, confirmed that a blip turning into a loss revokes an answer accepted during it and raised a repeated timestamp skipping the error check (P2) and an invalid frame hiding a track change or time going back (P2), both fixed fail closed. The owner chose a fifth round: it judged both resolved and raised a frame without a timestamp hiding a track change (P2), fixed by reading the track first; a narrow sixth round on that fix found nothing material.
